SAT Diagnostic

Reading and Writing · 14 questions · 16 min

diagnostic-rw-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The town council's decision to close the library on Sundays was met with ______ opposition: within a week, more than two thousand residents had signed a petition asking the council to reconsider.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. negligible
  2. hesitant
  3. widespread
  4. private
Answer C. The second half of the sentence describes thousands of residents signing a petition, so the opposition was broad: "widespread". "Negligible" and "hesitant" contradict the evidence, and a public petition is not "private".
diagnostic-rw-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
Early reviewers dismissed the novelist's first book as slight, but later critics have come to regard its spare, understated style as ______: a deliberate choice that gives its quiet scenes unusual emotional force.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. a weakness
  2. an accident
  3. a distraction
  4. a strength
Answer D. The colon explains the blank: the style is "a deliberate choice that gives its quiet scenes unusual emotional force", which is praise. So later critics see the style as "a strength", in contrast with the early reviewers.
diagnostic-rw-03 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Honeybees communicate the location of food through a "waggle dance." For decades, researchers assumed the dance was entirely instinctive. A 2023 study, however, found that young bees who never watched experienced dancers performed noticeably less precise dances, suggesting that bees refine the behaviour by observing others.
Which choice best describes the function of the third sentence in the overall structure of the text?
  1. It presents evidence that challenges a long-held assumption described earlier in the text.
  2. It explains why researchers were first interested in the waggle dance.
  3. It describes a method that later studies have shown to be unreliable.
  4. It summarises the main argument of the researchers mentioned in the second sentence.
Answer A. The second sentence gives the old assumption (the dance is entirely instinctive). The study, introduced with "however", shows learning plays a part, so it challenges that assumption.
diagnostic-rw-04 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
Some economists argue that a four-day working week raises productivity: rested employees, they say, accomplish as much in four days as they previously did in five, so output stays level while wellbeing improves.


Text 2
Trials of the four-day week have produced encouraging results, but most took place in office jobs where output is hard to measure. In settings such as hospitals or factories, where a shorter week simply means fewer hours of staffing, it is far less clear that the same amount of work would get done.
Based on the texts, how would the author of Text 2 most likely respond to the economists' claim in Text 1?
  1. By arguing that the trials described in Text 1 were poorly designed and their results should be ignored
  2. By suggesting that the claim may hold for some kinds of work but has not been shown to apply to all jobs
  3. By agreeing that productivity rises in every workplace that adopts a four-day week
  4. By claiming that employee wellbeing is more important than productivity
Answer B. Text 2 calls the results "encouraging" but notes they come mostly from office jobs and that it is "far less clear" for hospitals or factories. So the author accepts the claim may hold in some settings but doubts it applies everywhere.
diagnostic-rw-05 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
Mangrove forests grow along tropical coastlines where few other trees can survive the salty, waterlogged soil. Their tangled roots slow incoming waves, reducing coastal erosion, and shelter young fish that later move out to coral reefs. Because of these roles, scientists describe mangroves as a "nursery" for the ocean and a natural barrier for coastal communities.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Mangroves are difficult to grow outside the tropics.
  2. Coastal erosion is the most serious threat facing tropical coastlines.
  3. Coral reefs depend more on mangroves than on any other habitat.
  4. Mangroves provide important benefits to both marine life and coastal areas.
Answer D. The text lists two kinds of benefit (slowing waves to reduce erosion, and sheltering young fish) and sums them up as a "nursery" for the ocean and a "natural barrier" for communities. The other choices are either not stated or too narrow.
diagnostic-rw-06 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
A researcher claims that children who learn a musical instrument develop stronger working memory, the ability to hold and use information over a few seconds.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support the researcher's claim?
  1. Children randomly assigned to two years of instrument lessons improved more on working-memory tests than similar children assigned to art lessons.
  2. Many professional musicians began learning their instrument before age ten.
  3. Children who learn an instrument often say they enjoy their lessons.
  4. Adults who play an instrument tend to listen to more music than adults who do not.
Answer A. Only the finding about randomly assigned lessons links instrument learning to working memory, and with random assignment and a comparison group it suggests the lessons caused the improvement. The others say nothing about working memory.
diagnostic-rw-07 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 2
A team surveyed visitors to four national parks about how they travelled there. In every park, most visitors arrived by car, but the share arriving by public transport varied widely.


Visitors arriving by public transport
Park P: 4%
Park Q: 21%
Park R: 9%
Park S: 2%


The team concluded that parks with direct train links attract more public-transport visitors. Of the four parks, only Park Q has a direct train link.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the team's conclusion?
  1. Park S had the smallest share of visitors arriving by public transport.
  2. Park Q, the only park with a direct train link, had a far higher share of public-transport visitors than any other park.
  3. Most visitors to every park arrived by car.
  4. Parks P and R together had a larger share of public-transport visitors than Park S.
Answer B. The conclusion is about train links. Park Q is the only park with one, and its 21% is far above the others (2% to 9%), which is the data that supports the conclusion.
diagnostic-rw-08 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
In laboratory tests, a new drought-resistant variety of wheat produced yields similar to standard wheat when watered normally, and much higher yields when water was scarce. However, the new variety needs a soil nutrient that is low in many of the dry regions where it would be planted. Therefore, without ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. more laboratory testing, the variety's yields under normal watering will remain unknown.
  2. rainfall, standard wheat will always outperform the new variety.
  3. added fertiliser, the variety may not deliver its advantage in the places that need it most.
  4. changes to the variety, it cannot be grown in any region at all.
Answer C. The problem raised is that dry regions lack a nutrient the variety needs. So unless the nutrient is supplied (fertiliser), its drought advantage may not appear where droughts happen. The option about laboratory testing contradicts the text, which already reports yields under normal watering.
diagnostic-rw-09 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 1
Each of the paintings in the museum's new gallery ______ by an artist who was born in the city.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. were created
  2. was created
  3. are created
  4. have been created
Answer B. The subject is "Each", which is singular ("of the paintings" is a phrase in between). The singular verb "was created" agrees with it.
diagnostic-rw-10 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The bridge was designed to carry 20,000 vehicles a ______ by its tenth year, it was carrying nearly twice that number.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. day,
  2. day
  3. day;
  4. day, and,
Answer C. Both parts are complete sentences, so they need a semicolon (or a full stop) between them. A comma alone creates a comma splice, and no punctuation creates a run-on.
diagnostic-rw-11 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The expedition's leader, a geologist who had spent two decades mapping the ______ insisted on checking every rock sample herself.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. region;
  2. region:
  3. region
  4. region,
Answer D. The phrase "a geologist who had spent two decades mapping the region" interrupts the sentence, so it must be closed with a comma to match the comma after "leader".
diagnostic-rw-12 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
After months of negotiation, the two companies finally agreed to share ______ research facilities, a move that analysts expect will cut costs for both.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. their
  2. its
  3. it's
  4. they're
Answer A. The facilities belong to "the two companies", a plural noun, so the plural possessive "their" is needed. "Its" is singular, and "it's" and "they're" are contractions.
diagnostic-rw-13 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 1
Electric scooters are cheap to rent and easy to park. ______, many cities have struggled with scooters left blocking pavements and doorways.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. However
  2. For example
  3. Similarly
  4. As a result
Answer A. The second sentence describes a problem that contrasts with the advantages in the first, so a contrast word, "However", fits.
diagnostic-rw-14 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Great Green Wall is a project to restore land across Africa's Sahel region.
• It was launched in 2007 by the African Union.
• Its goal is to restore 100 million hectares of degraded land by 2030.
• By 2020, about 4 million hectares (roughly 4% of the goal) had been restored.
The student wants to emphasise how much work remains for the project to meet its goal. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Great Green Wall, launched by the African Union in 2007, aims to restore land across the Sahel region.
  2. By 2020, the Great Green Wall had restored only about 4% of the 100 million hectares it aims to restore by 2030.
  3. The Great Green Wall is a land-restoration project in Africa's Sahel region.
  4. Launched in 2007, the Great Green Wall has a goal for the year 2030.
Answer B. Only the sentence about 2020 sets what has been done (about 4%) against the goal (100 million hectares by 2030), which shows how much remains.

Math · 12 questions · 18 min

diagnostic-m-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $5x - 8 = 3x + 6$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $-7$
  2. $1$
  3. $7$
  4. $14$
Answer C. Subtract $3x$ and add $8$ to both sides: $2x = 14$, so $x = 7$.
diagnostic-m-02 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
A gym charges a joining fee plus a fixed amount per month. The total cost, in dollars, of $m$ months of membership is given by $C(m) = 25m + 40$. What does the number $40$ represent?
  1. The joining fee
  2. The cost per month
  3. The total cost of one month
  4. The number of months
Answer A. When $m = 0$ (no months yet), $C = 40$: the amount paid before any monthly charge, which is the joining fee. $25$ is the cost per month.
diagnostic-m-03 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
A jacket originally priced at $\$80$ is on sale for $15\%$ off. What is the sale price, in dollars?

Type-in answer: 68 or 68.00 or 68.0

Answer 68. $15\%$ of $80$ is $12$, so the sale price is $80 - 12 = 68$ dollars.
diagnostic-m-04 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A printer prints $45$ pages in $3$ minutes. At this rate, how many minutes will it take to print $270$ pages?
  1. $12$
  2. $15$
  3. $18$
  4. $20$
Answer C. The rate is $45 \div 3 = 15$ pages per minute, so $270$ pages take $270 \div 15 = 18$ minutes.
diagnostic-m-05 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$2x + y = 11$
$x - y = 1$


What is the solution $(x, y)$ to the system of equations above?
  1. $(3, 5)$
  2. $(4, 3)$
  3. $(5, 1)$
  4. $(6, 5)$
Answer B. Add the equations: $3x = 12$, so $x = 4$. Then $y = x - 1 = 3$.
diagnostic-m-06 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $(2x + 3)(x - 4)$?
  1. $2x^2 - 5x - 12$
  2. $2x^2 + 5x - 12$
  3. $2x^2 - 8x - 12$
  4. $2x^2 - 11x + 12$
Answer A. Expand: $2x \cdot x + 2x(-4) + 3x + 3(-4) = 2x^2 - 8x + 3x - 12 = 2x^2 - 5x - 12$.
diagnostic-m-07 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
A right triangle has legs of length $9$ and $12$. What is the length of its hypotenuse?

Type-in answer: 15

Answer 15. By Pythagoras, the hypotenuse is $\sqrt{9^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{81 + 144} = \sqrt{225} = 15$.
diagnostic-m-08 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 2
The data set $3, 7, 7, 10, 13$ has a mean of $8$. If the value $13$ is replaced by $23$, which of the following is true?
  1. Both the mean and the median increase.
  2. The mean increases and the median stays the same.
  3. The mean stays the same and the median increases.
  4. Neither the mean nor the median changes.
Answer B. The total rises by $10$, so the mean rises to $10$. The middle value is still $7$, so the median does not change.
diagnostic-m-09 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 2(x - 3)^2 + 5$. What is the minimum value of $f(x)$?
  1. $-5$
  2. $2$
  3. $3$
  4. $5$
Answer D. $(x - 3)^2 \ge 0$, so $f(x) \ge 5$, with equality when $x = 3$. The minimum value is $5$ (the $y$-coordinate of the vertex $(3, 5)$).
diagnostic-m-10 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle in the $xy$-plane has equation $x^2 + y^2 - 6x + 4y = 12$. What is the radius of the circle?
  1. $\sqrt{12}$
  2. $5$
  3. $12$
  4. $25$
Answer B. Complete the square: $(x - 3)^2 - 9 + (y + 2)^2 - 4 = 12$, so $(x - 3)^2 + (y + 2)^2 = 25$. The radius is $\sqrt{25} = 5$.
diagnostic-m-11 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
What is the positive solution to the equation $x^2 - 2x - 35 = 0$?

Type-in answer: 7

Answer 7. Factor: $(x - 7)(x + 5) = 0$, so $x = 7$ or $x = -5$. The positive solution is $7$.
diagnostic-m-12 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
A survey asked 200 students whether they walk or take the bus to school.


WalkBusTotal
Year 103664100
Year 115446100
Total90110200



If a student who walks is chosen at random, what is the probability that the student is in Year 11?
  1. $\dfrac{27}{100}$
  2. $\dfrac{9}{20}$
  3. $\dfrac{27}{50}$
  4. $\dfrac{3}{5}$
Answer D. Only the $90$ walkers count. Of them, $54$ are in Year 11, so the probability is $\frac{54}{90} = \frac{3}{5}$.

SAT Practice Test 1

Reading and Writing · Module 1 · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-1-rw1-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The chef's new menu was deliberately ______: instead of the dozens of dishes the restaurant once offered, it listed only six, each made with ingredients from nearby farms.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. concise
  2. extensive
  3. expensive
  4. traditional
Answer A. The colon explains the blank: the menu went from dozens of dishes to six. A short, focused menu is "concise".
mock-1-rw1-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
Although the bridge looked fragile, engineers found it remarkably ______: it had survived two major floods and decades of heavy traffic with only minor repairs.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. ornate
  2. durable
  3. temporary
  4. crowded
Answer B. "Although" sets up a contrast with "fragile", and the evidence (surviving floods and traffic) shows the bridge lasts. "Durable" means able to withstand wear.
mock-1-rw1-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
Historians have long ______ the role of women in the early printing trade. Recent research in guild records shows that widows frequently ran print shops and trained apprentices, a contribution earlier accounts barely mention.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. exaggerated
  2. celebrated
  3. overlooked
  4. documented
Answer C. The second sentence says earlier accounts "barely mention" women's contribution, so historians have "overlooked" it. "Celebrated" and "documented" contradict this, and "exaggerated" means the opposite.
mock-1-rw1-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The economist's forecast was ______: it predicted modest growth but surrounded that prediction with so many qualifications and alternative scenarios that almost any outcome could be said to fit it.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. unprecedented
  2. pessimistic
  3. erroneous
  4. equivocal
Answer D. A forecast hedged with so many qualifications that almost any outcome fits it is "equivocal" (open to more than one interpretation). Nothing suggests it was gloomy, wrong or unprecedented.
mock-1-rw1-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 1
Sea otters often float on their backs and use a flat rock resting on their chests as an anvil, striking shellfish against it until the shells crack. Some otters keep a favourite rock tucked in a fold of skin under their forelegs, carrying it from one meal to the next.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To describe how sea otters use rocks to open their food
  2. To compare sea otters with other tool-using animals
  3. To explain why sea otters float on their backs
  4. To argue that sea otters are the most intelligent marine mammals
Answer A. Both sentences describe otters using rocks as anvils to crack shellfish, including carrying a favourite rock. No other animals are compared and no argument about intelligence is made.
mock-1-rw1-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
For years, city planners assumed that adding lanes to a congested road would reduce traffic. Studies of dozens of expansions have found the opposite: within a few years, the new lanes fill with drivers who previously took other routes or travelled at other times. Planners call this effect "induced demand."
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It describes a problem, then proposes several possible solutions.
  2. It presents a common assumption, then describes evidence that contradicts it and names the effect.
  3. It explains a scientific term, then gives its history.
  4. It compares two cities' approaches to reducing traffic.
Answer B. The text states what planners assumed, gives studies showing "the opposite", and ends by naming the effect ("induced demand").
mock-1-rw1-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
In her 1962 memoir, the architect describes her first commission, a small library in a mountain town, in loving detail: the creak of its floorboards, the smell of pine, the way afternoon light fell across the reading tables. Yet she devotes only a single sentence to the vast government building that made her famous.
Which choice best describes the function of the second sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It explains why the architect stopped designing libraries.
  2. It suggests that the government building was poorly designed.
  3. It provides a contrast that highlights how much more attention the architect gives to her first commission.
  4. It introduces a criticism of the memoir made by other architects.
Answer C. "Yet" signals a contrast: the small library gets loving detail, while her most famous building gets one sentence. The contrast emphasises the attention she gives the library. Nothing is said about quality or critics.
mock-1-rw1-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Many nutritionists recommend eating breakfast every day, arguing that a morning meal improves concentration and helps people avoid overeating later.


Text 2
In a large study, adults who were randomly assigned to skip breakfast did not eat more at lunch or dinner than those who ate breakfast, and their performance on attention tests was the same. The study's authors suggest that the benefits of breakfast may depend more on the individual than on any general rule.
Based on the texts, how would the authors of the study in Text 2 most likely respond to the nutritionists' recommendation in Text 1?
  1. They would agree that breakfast improves concentration for most adults.
  2. They would suggest that nutritionists should study children instead of adults.
  3. They would claim that skipping breakfast is healthier than eating it.
  4. They would argue that the recommendation may not apply to everyone, since their results did not show the claimed benefits.
Answer D. The study found no difference in later eating or attention, and its authors say benefits "may depend more on the individual than on any general rule". So they would question a recommendation for everyone, without claiming skipping breakfast is better.
mock-1-rw1-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
Before refrigerators, many households kept food cold in an "icebox", a wooden cabinet lined with tin or zinc and packed with a large block of ice. Ice was cut from frozen lakes in winter, stored in insulated ice houses, and delivered door to door through the summer. The trade employed tens of thousands of workers until electric refrigerators became affordable in the 1930s.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Electric refrigerators were invented in the 1930s.
  2. Before electric refrigerators, a large industry supplied ice so households could keep food cold.
  3. Ice houses were the most important buildings in early towns.
  4. Wooden cabinets were better at keeping food cold than electric refrigerators.
Answer B. The text describes iceboxes, how ice was harvested, stored and delivered, and how many people the trade employed until refrigerators replaced it. Together that is the ice industry that kept food cold before refrigerators.
mock-1-rw1-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from a 1913 short story. Maria has just arrived in a city for the first time.


Everything was taller than she had imagined and louder than she had feared. She stood at the corner with her single suitcase, letting the crowd part around her like water around a stone, and for a long moment she could not decide whether she wanted to laugh or to go straight back to the station.
According to the text, what is true about Maria?
  1. She is waiting for a friend to meet her.
  2. She has visited the city many times before.
  3. She is unsure how she feels about the city.
  4. She is angry that people are ignoring her.
Answer C. She "could not decide whether she wanted to laugh or to go straight back to the station", which shows mixed, uncertain feelings. The text says this is her first time in a city.
mock-1-rw1-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
A historian claims that the spread of public libraries in the late nineteenth century increased reading among working-class adults, not just among the wealthy.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support the historian's claim?
  1. Some public libraries charged a small fee to borrow new novels.
  2. Many public libraries built in the period had grand entrances and marble floors.
  3. Wealthy families in the period often owned large private libraries.
  4. Library membership records from the period show that most new members listed factory, dock or domestic work as their occupation.
Answer D. The claim is about working-class adults reading more thanks to public libraries. Records showing that most new members had working-class jobs connect the libraries directly to that group.
mock-1-rw1-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Ecologist Rosa Delgado studied a meadow where a non-native plant had spread. She hypothesised that the plant was reducing the number of native bee species, not because bees avoided it, but because it crowded out the native flowers those bees depend on.
Which finding, if true, would most strongly support Delgado's hypothesis?
  1. Areas of the meadow where the non-native plant was removed and native flowers regrew saw native bee species return.
  2. Native bees were frequently observed visiting the non-native plant's flowers.
  3. The non-native plant flowers earlier in spring than most native plants.
  4. Non-native bees in other regions prefer native flowers to non-native ones.
Answer A. The hypothesis links bee decline to the loss of native flowers. If bees return when native flowers regrow after the plant is removed, the loss of those flowers is the likely cause. Bees visiting the plant does not show what drives the decline.
mock-1-rw1-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 1
A school surveyed students about how they usually get to school.


MethodPercentage of students
Walk35%
Bus40%
Car18%
Bicycle7%



The survey found that the most common way students get to school is ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to complete the statement?
  1. by bus.
  2. on foot.
  3. by car.
  4. by bicycle.
Answer A. The bus has the highest percentage in the table (40%), so it is the most common method.
mock-1-rw1-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
Researchers measured average daily screen time and average nightly sleep for teenagers in four groups.


GroupScreen time (hours)Sleep (hours)
12.18.4
23.87.9
35.27.1
46.96.6



The researchers argue that their data are consistent with the idea that more screen time is associated with less sleep, but they caution that the data alone cannot show that screens cause the loss of sleep.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the researchers' argument?
  1. Group 1 had the least screen time and the most sleep.
  2. As average screen time increased from group to group, average sleep decreased.
  3. Group 4's screen time was more than three times group 1's.
  4. Every group averaged more than six hours of sleep.
Answer B. The argument is about an association: more screen time going with less sleep. Only the statement describing the pattern across all four groups (screen time rising as sleep falls) supports it. The others describe single groups.
mock-1-rw1-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
Glass frogs have transparent skin on their undersides. Researchers recently discovered that when a glass frog sleeps, it stores most of its red blood cells in its liver, making its body almost completely see-through. Because predators in the frogs' forest habitat often hunt by sight, this suggests that ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. the liver is the largest organ in a glass frog's body.
  2. glass frogs are more active at night than during the day.
  3. hiding red blood cells may help glass frogs avoid being seen while they sleep.
  4. predators in the forest cannot see the colour red.
Answer C. Predators hunt by sight, and storing blood in the liver makes a sleeping frog nearly invisible. The logical conclusion is that this helps it avoid predators while asleep.
mock-1-rw1-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Medieval manuscripts were often written on parchment made from animal skin, which was expensive. Scribes sometimes scraped old texts off parchment and wrote new ones over them. Using special imaging, researchers can now read the faint traces of the erased writing. Because the erased texts were usually ones that scribes no longer considered valuable, the recovered writing is likely to include ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. works that were widely copied and read at the time.
  2. writing that the scribes themselves had composed.
  3. only religious texts.
  4. texts that did not survive anywhere else.
Answer D. Texts scribes no longer valued were scraped off rather than copied, so they are the ones least likely to survive elsewhere. Widely copied works are the opposite.
mock-1-rw1-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 1
The museum's newest exhibit features over two hundred ______ many of which have never been shown to the public.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. photographs,
  2. photographs;
  3. photographs.
  4. photographs
Answer A. "Many of which have never been shown" is not a complete sentence, so it cannot follow a semicolon or full stop. It adds detail to "photographs" and is joined with a comma.
mock-1-rw1-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The first public demonstration of television took place in London in ______ the inventor, John Logie Baird, showed moving images of a ventriloquist's dummy to a group of scientists.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. 1926
  2. 1926, and
  3. 1926 and
  4. 1926,
Answer B. Two complete sentences are being joined, so they need a comma plus a conjunction ("1926, and"). A comma alone is a comma splice; no punctuation is a run-on.
mock-1-rw1-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The recipe calls for three ______ flour, sugar and cocoa powder.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. ingredients,
  2. ingredients;
  3. ingredients:
  4. ingredients
Answer C. A colon introduces a list after a complete sentence ("The recipe calls for three ingredients"). A semicolon needs complete sentences on both sides.
mock-1-rw1-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
Marine biologist Asha ______ who has spent fifteen years tracking whale sharks off the coast of Mozambique, believes the animals return to the same feeding grounds each year.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. Nkemelu:
  2. Nkemelu
  3. Nkemelu;
  4. Nkemelu,
Answer D. The clause "who has spent fifteen years..." is extra information about Nkemelu and ends with a comma before "believes", so it must also start with a comma.
mock-1-rw1-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 1
The team of volunteers ______ every weekend to clear litter from the riverbank.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. meet
  2. have met
  3. are meeting
  4. meets
Answer D. The subject is "team", a singular noun ("of volunteers" describes it), so it takes the singular verb "meets".
mock-1-rw1-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
By the time the rescue boat arrived, the stranded hikers ______ on the small island for nearly two days.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. had been waiting
  2. will wait
  3. wait
  4. are waiting
Answer A. The waiting happened before another past event (the boat arriving), so the past perfect continuous "had been waiting" is needed.
mock-1-rw1-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Walking through the market at dawn, ______
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. the smell of fresh bread drew the tourists toward a small bakery.
  2. the tourists were drawn toward a small bakery by the smell of fresh bread.
  3. a small bakery drew the tourists in with the smell of fresh bread.
  4. fresh bread was smelled by the tourists near a small bakery.
Answer B. "Walking through the market at dawn" must describe whoever is walking, so the tourists must come right after the comma. In the other options the smell, the bakery or the bread would be doing the walking.
mock-1-rw1-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 1
The first part of the hike follows a wide, gently sloping path. ______, the final kilometre is steep and rocky, and hikers often need to use their hands.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. For instance
  2. Similarly
  3. By contrast
  4. Therefore
Answer C. The final kilometre (steep, rocky) is the opposite of the first part (wide, gentle), so a contrast transition fits.
mock-1-rw1-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
Honey found in ancient Egyptian tombs has been tasted by archaeologists and found to be perfectly edible. Honey's low moisture and natural acidity make it hostile to bacteria. ______, it can last for thousands of years if sealed properly.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Nevertheless
  2. As a result
  3. In other words
  4. Meanwhile
Answer B. Honey being hostile to bacteria is the reason it can last so long, so the third sentence is a result of the second.
mock-1-rw1-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Atacama Desert in Chile is one of the driest places on Earth.
• Some weather stations there have never recorded rain.
• Its clear, dry skies make it ideal for astronomy.
• Several of the world's largest telescopes are built there.
The student wants to explain why the Atacama Desert attracts astronomers. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Atacama Desert in Chile is one of the driest places on Earth.
  2. Some weather stations in the Atacama Desert have never recorded rain.
  3. Because its skies are so clear and dry, the Atacama Desert is home to several of the world's largest telescopes.
  4. Chile is home to the Atacama Desert, where it rarely rains.
Answer C. The goal is to explain the attraction for astronomers. Only the sentence linking clear, dry skies to the telescopes built there does that.
mock-1-rw1-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Bioluminescence is light produced by living organisms.
• Fireflies use it to attract mates.
• The anglerfish uses a glowing lure to attract prey.
• Some squid use it to match light from above and hide their shadows from predators.
The student wants to emphasise that organisms use bioluminescence for different purposes. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Bioluminescence, light produced by living organisms, is found in fireflies and anglerfish.
  2. Some squid use bioluminescence to hide their shadows.
  3. The anglerfish has a glowing lure that it uses to attract prey.
  4. Organisms use bioluminescence in different ways: fireflies to attract mates, anglerfish to lure prey and some squid to hide from predators.
Answer D. The goal is several different purposes. Only the sentence naming three uses (attracting mates, luring prey, hiding) shows that variety.

  2. To argue that the road should be closed every spring
  3. To describe a local effort to help toads reach their breeding pond safely
  4. To compare toads with other migrating animals

mock-1-rw2e-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Most people picture deserts as hot. But the largest desert on Earth is Antarctica. A desert is defined by how little precipitation it receives, not by its temperature, and Antarctica's interior receives less than 5 centimetres of snow a year.
Which choice best describes the function of the third sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It questions whether Antarctica should be called a desert.
  2. It describes the wildlife that lives in Antarctica.
  3. It gives a second example of a hot desert.
  4. It explains the definition that makes the surprising claim in the second sentence true.
Answer D. The second sentence makes a surprising claim (Antarctica is the largest desert). The third explains why: deserts are defined by precipitation, and Antarctica gets very little.
mock-1-rw2e-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from a 1908 novel. Anne, a young orphan, has just arrived at a farm.


Anne looked around the kitchen with shining eyes. "Oh, I'm so glad I'm going to stay here," she said, although no one had yet told her that she would. "I've never had a real home before, and this looks exactly like the sort of place I always imagined."
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. A character reacts with joy to a new place and explains why it matters to her.
  2. A character argues with another character about where she will live.
  3. A narrator describes a farm in detail and then explains its history.
  4. A character remembers a home she lived in long ago.
Answer A. Anne looks around "with shining eyes", says she is glad to stay, and explains she has never had a real home, so the passage shows her joy and the reason for it.
mock-1-rw2e-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Some teachers argue that students learn best when classrooms are silent, because noise makes it harder to concentrate.


Text 2
A study of primary school pupils found that low background noise, like quiet chatter, did not reduce test scores, while loud noise, such as traffic, did. The researchers concluded that it is the volume of noise, rather than any noise at all, that matters.
Based on the texts, how would the researchers in Text 2 most likely respond to the teachers in Text 1?
  1. By agreeing that any noise at all harms learning
  2. By suggesting that only loud noise, not all noise, makes concentration harder
  3. By arguing that classrooms should be as loud as possible
  4. By claiming that noise has no effect on learning
Answer B. The study found quiet noise did not lower scores but loud noise did, so the researchers would qualify the teachers' view: only loud noise is the problem.
mock-1-rw2e-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Coral reefs are found only in Australia.
  2. The Great Barrier Reef is enormous and full of life.
  3. Most fish species live on the Great Barrier Reef.
  4. Scientists use space photographs to study coral reefs.
Answer B. Each detail (largest reef system, over 2,300 km, thousands of species, visible from space) shows how large and full of life the reef is.

Our school's chess club meets every ______ new members are always welcome.

  1. Tuesday,
  2. Tuesday
  3. Tuesday;
  4. Tuesday, and,
Answer C. Two complete sentences are joined, so a semicolon is needed. A comma alone is a comma splice, and no punctuation is a run-on.

mock-1-rw2e-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2

  1. they're
  2. it's
  3. their's
  4. its
Answer D. The instruments belong to "the orchestra", treated here as a single group, so the possessive "its" is used. "It's" means "it is".

  4. Similarly

Reading and Writing · Module 2 (harder) · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-1-rw2h-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The committee's report was notably ______: rather than recommending a single course of action, it laid out the strengths and weaknesses of each option and left the final decision to the council.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. partisan
  2. hasty
  3. dismissive
  4. even-handed
Answer D. Setting out strengths and weaknesses of every option without pushing one is balanced, or "even-handed". "Partisan" means taking a side, the opposite.
mock-1-rw2h-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Critics initially praised the documentary for its candour, but later investigations revealed that several of its most striking scenes had been staged. In retrospect, the film's apparent honesty was largely ______.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. illusory
  2. inadvertent
  3. redundant
  4. unassailable
Answer A. Staged scenes mean the honesty only seemed real, so it was "illusory". "Unassailable" means beyond challenge, which the investigations contradict.
mock-1-rw2h-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Rather than abandoning traditional techniques, the ceramicist ______ them, adapting centuries-old glazing methods to work with materials that were unavailable to earlier potters.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. repudiated
  2. reinvented
  3. imitated
  4. neglected
Answer B. She did not abandon the techniques but adapted them for new materials, so she "reinvented" them. "Repudiated" and "neglected" mean rejecting or ignoring them, and "imitated" misses the adaptation.
mock-1-rw2h-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The senator's speech was so ______ that reporters struggled to identify a single concrete proposal among its many appeals to unity, progress and shared values.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. incisive
  2. succinct
  3. nebulous
  4. inflammatory
Answer C. A speech with no concrete proposal, only broad appeals, is vague: "nebulous". "Incisive" and "succinct" suggest sharpness and brevity, the opposite.
mock-1-rw2h-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1920 poem. The speaker describes an old house.


The rooms are full of clocks that do not run,
Of chairs that wait for guests who never come;
The windows hold the colour of the sun
But let no warmth inside this patient home.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It describes the house's history from its building to the present.
  2. It lists details of the house that together suggest stillness and absence.
  3. It contrasts the house's cheerful past with its gloomy present.
  4. It explains why the house's owners left.
Answer B. Stopped clocks, chairs waiting for guests who never come and windows that let in no warmth are a list of details creating a mood of stillness and absence. No history, past or explanation is given.
mock-1-rw2h-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
It is tempting to credit the printing press alone with the spread of literacy in early modern Europe. Yet in several regions, literacy rates rose sharply only decades after printing arrived, once schooling expanded and books became cheap enough for ordinary households. The press, in other words, made widespread literacy possible; it did not by itself make it happen.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To describe how early printing presses worked
  2. To show that the printing press slowed the spread of literacy
  3. To argue that the printing press's role in spreading literacy has been overstated
  4. To explain why books in early modern Europe were expensive
Answer C. The text calls crediting the press "alone" tempting, then shows literacy rose only when other factors appeared, concluding the press made literacy possible but did not cause it alone. That qualifies the press's role; it never says the press slowed literacy.
mock-1-rw2h-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Biologists once classified fungi as plants because both are rooted in place and grow from the ground. Genetic studies have since shown that fungi are more closely related to animals than to plants. Like animals, fungi cannot make their own food and must absorb nutrients from other organisms, and their cell walls contain chitin, a substance also found in insect shells.
Which choice best describes the function of the third sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It describes how insects use chitin to build their shells.
  2. It explains why biologists first classified fungi as plants.
  3. It challenges the genetic studies mentioned in the second sentence.
  4. It gives features that fungi share with animals, supporting the finding described in the second sentence.
Answer D. The second sentence says fungi are closer to animals; the third lists shared features (absorbing food, chitin) that back this up. The insect detail is only an example of chitin.
mock-1-rw2h-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
The decline of honeybee colonies is often blamed on a single cause, such as a particular pesticide. But beekeepers' records suggest that colonies rarely fail for one reason. Parasites, poor nutrition and pesticide exposure each weaken bees, and colonies collapse when several stresses occur together.


Text 2
In a controlled experiment, researchers exposed colonies to low doses of a common pesticide. Exposed colonies that were also infested with mites suffered heavy losses, while exposed colonies without mites did about as well as unexposed ones.
Based on the texts, how would the author of Text 1 most likely view the results of the experiment in Text 2?
  1. As consistent with the view that colonies collapse when several stresses combine
  2. As evidence that the pesticide is the single main cause of colony decline
  3. As proof that mites are harmless to honeybees
  4. As irrelevant, because experiments cannot show what happens to real colonies
Answer A. In Text 2, the pesticide hurt colonies only when mites were also present: two stresses together caused losses. That matches Text 1's view that collapse comes from combined stresses, not a single cause.
mock-1-rw2h-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from Jane Austen's 1813 novel Pride and Prejudice.


Mr. Bennet was so odd a mixture of quick parts, sarcastic humour, reserve and caprice, that the experience of three-and-twenty years had been insufficient to make his wife understand his character.
Which choice best describes Mr. Bennet's character as presented in the text?
  1. He is complicated enough that even his wife has not understood him after many years.
  2. He is honest and straightforward with his family.
  3. He is unhappy because his wife does not respect him.
  4. He has only recently married.
Answer A. His "odd mixture" of traits means twenty-three years have not been enough for his wife to understand him, so he is complicated and hard to read. "Three-and-twenty years" also shows the marriage is long.
mock-1-rw2h-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 3
Sociologist Dalia Haddad studied neighbourhood libraries in three cities. She found that libraries offering services beyond lending books, such as job-search help, language classes and spaces for community meetings, had more visitors than libraries that focused on books alone. Haddad argues that libraries are increasingly valued less as storehouses of books than as public spaces where people can gain skills and connect with one another.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Most people no longer read books from libraries.
  2. Libraries that offer community services attract more visitors, suggesting people value libraries as public spaces.
  3. Libraries should stop lending books and offer only classes.
  4. Haddad's study included more cities than earlier studies of libraries.
Answer B. The text presents Haddad's finding (more visitors to libraries with extra services) and her argument (libraries valued as public spaces). It never says people stopped reading or that lending should end.
mock-1-rw2h-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1919 short story. The narrator describes his grandmother.


She kept the best garden on the street and baked bread every Saturday, but what people remembered about her was something else. She had a way of listening that made you feel you were the only person in the world who had ever said anything interesting. Visitors who came to call for ten minutes often stayed for two hours, and left looking surprised at the time.
The narrator claims that the grandmother's way of listening made people feel interesting. Which quotation from the text most effectively illustrates this claim?
  1. "baked bread every Saturday"
  2. "She kept the best garden on the street"
  3. "Visitors who came to call for ten minutes often stayed for two hours, and left looking surprised at the time."
  4. "what people remembered about her was something else"
Answer C. Visitors staying two hours instead of ten minutes shows the effect of her listening: people kept talking because she made them feel interesting. The garden and the bread are about other things, and "something else" does not say what.
mock-1-rw2h-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Linguist Tomás Reyes hypothesises that children learning two languages at once do not become confused between them, as some parents fear, but instead learn early to keep the languages separate, using each with the people who speak it.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Reyes's hypothesis?
  1. Children raised with one language learn a second language more slowly as teenagers.
  2. Children raised with two languages sometimes mix words from both in a single sentence.
  3. Parents who speak two languages often worry about confusing their children.
  4. Two-year-olds raised with two languages consistently used each language with the parent who spoke it to them.
Answer D. The hypothesis is that young bilingual children keep the languages apart, using each with its speakers. Two-year-olds matching each language to the parent who speaks it is exactly that. Mixing words points the other way.
mock-1-rw2h-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 2
A researcher compared four kinds of light bulb.
