Which choice best describes data from the table that support the researcher's conclusion?
  1. The LED bulb lasts 25,000 hours, the longest of the four.
  2. The LED bulb gives as much light as the incandescent bulb while using the least power of the four.
  3. The halogen bulb uses less power than the incandescent bulb.
  4. The CFL bulb gives 800 lumens of light.
Answer B. The conclusion is about bright light and little electricity. The LED gives 800 lumens (as bright as the brightest) using only 9 watts (the least). Lifespan, though impressive, is not part of the conclusion.
mock-1-rw2h-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
Economists tracked average weekly hours worked and average output per hour in one industry.


YearWeekly hoursOutput per hour (units)
20004420
20054223
20104026
20153830



Some managers in the industry worried that shorter working weeks would lead to less total output per worker. The data, however, suggest that this concern may be unfounded, since ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to complete the statement?
  1. output per hour was highest in 2015.
  2. weekly hours fell from 44 in 2000 to 38 in 2015.
  3. output per hour rose enough that weekly output per worker increased from 880 units in 2000 to 1,140 units in 2015.
  4. weekly hours fell by the same amount every five years.
Answer C. The worry is about total output per worker. Weekly output is hours times output per hour: 44 × 20 = 880 in 2000 and 38 × 30 = 1,140 in 2015, so total output rose despite shorter weeks. Hours falling alone would support the worry, not answer it.
mock-1-rw2h-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
When the ancient city of Pompeii was buried by volcanic ash in 79 CE, many everyday objects were preserved, including graffiti scratched into walls. Much of this graffiti consists of jokes, election slogans and messages between friends, written in an informal Latin that differs in spelling and grammar from the polished Latin of surviving literary works. Because most of the Latin that survives from the period was written by educated authors, the Pompeii graffiti is especially valuable to historians because it ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. contains the oldest surviving examples of Latin poetry.
  2. shows that most people in Pompeii could not read.
  3. proves that literary Latin was never spoken.
  4. offers evidence of how ordinary people in the city actually wrote.
Answer D. Surviving Latin mostly comes from educated authors, while the graffiti is everyday, informal writing. So its value lies in showing how ordinary people wrote. It does not show who could read or prove anything about speech.
mock-1-rw2h-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Some trees in dry regions shed their leaves during the hottest months rather than in winter. Leaves lose water through tiny pores as they take in carbon dioxide for photosynthesis. Without leaves, a tree loses much less water, but it also cannot photosynthesise. Researchers studying these trees should therefore expect that during the hottest months, the trees ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. grow little, if at all, while conserving water.
  2. photosynthesise more than at any other time of year.
  3. lose more water than trees that keep their leaves.
  4. take in more carbon dioxide than in winter.
Answer A. In the hottest months the trees are leafless, so they lose little water but cannot photosynthesise. With no photosynthesis there is little energy to grow. The other options contradict the text.
mock-1-rw2h-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The documentary covers three ______ the building of the canal, the epidemic that halted its construction, and the engineer who finally completed it.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. topics
  2. topics;
  3. topics:
  4. topics, which are
Answer C. A complete sentence ("The documentary covers three topics") introduces a list, so a colon is right. A semicolon would need a complete sentence after it.
mock-1-rw2h-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The novelist rarely gave ______ when she did, she spoke at length about the farmers and miners whose stories had shaped her books.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. interviews but
  2. interviews but,
  3. interviews, but,
  4. interviews, but
Answer D. Two complete sentences joined by "but" need a comma before "but" and nothing after it, since "when she did" belongs to the second clause.
mock-1-rw2h-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The researchers at the ______ led by astrophysicist Kenji Watanabe, detected the faint signal after months of observations.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. observatory,
  2. observatory
  3. observatory;
  4. observatory:
Answer A. The phrase "led by astrophysicist Kenji Watanabe" is extra information about the researchers, and it is closed by the comma after "Watanabe". So it must open with a comma too. A semicolon or colon would cut the subject off from its verb, "detected".
mock-1-rw2h-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
Many of the artist's early paintings were lost in a ______ the few that survived now hang in museums on three continents.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. fire, however,
  2. fire; however,
  3. fire however
  4. fire; however
Answer B. "However" begins a new complete sentence here, so it needs a semicolon before it (between the two sentences) and a comma after it.
mock-1-rw2h-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
Neither the coach nor the players ______ expected the team to reach the final.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. has
  2. had
  3. was
  4. is
Answer B. With "neither... nor", the verb agrees with the nearer subject, "players", which is plural. "Had expected" works for a plural subject; "has" and "was" are singular, and "is expected" changes the meaning.
mock-1-rw2h-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
The collection of letters, which includes correspondence from three generations of the family, ______ donated to the archive last spring.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. have been
  2. were
  3. was
  4. are
Answer C. The subject is "collection", which is singular; "of letters" and the "which" clause only describe it. So the singular "was" is needed.
mock-1-rw2h-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Unlike the city's older bridges, whose stone arches required decades to build, ______
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. it took less than two years to assemble the new bridge from prefabricated steel sections.
  2. engineers assembled the new bridge from prefabricated steel sections in under two years.
  3. prefabricated steel sections allowed the new bridge to be assembled in under two years.
  4. the new bridge was assembled from prefabricated steel sections in under two years.
Answer D. "Unlike the city's older bridges" compares bridges, so a bridge must follow the comma. Only "the new bridge was assembled..." makes the comparison bridge to bridge.
mock-1-rw2h-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
Early weather forecasts relied mainly on observations from a few weather stations. ______, forecasters can draw on data from satellites, radar and thousands of automated sensors.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Today
  2. Similarly
  3. As a result
  4. In conclusion
Answer A. The text contrasts early forecasting with how it works now, so a time transition, "Today", fits. Nothing in the second sentence results from the first.
mock-1-rw2h-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 3
The playwright's early comedies were commercial successes, filling theatres for months. Critics, ______, dismissed them as light entertainment, and it was not until her later, darker plays that she won serious critical attention.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. however
  2. therefore
  3. for example
  4. likewise
Answer A. Critics dismissing the plays contrasts with their commercial success, so "however" fits. "Therefore" would wrongly make the dismissal a result of the success.
mock-1-rw2h-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Svalbard Global Seed Vault is in Norway, inside a mountain on an Arctic island.
• It stores more than one million seed samples from almost every country.
• Its purpose is to protect crop diversity against disasters, wars and climate change.
• In 2015, researchers in Syria withdrew seeds to replace a seed bank damaged by war.
The student wants to give an example showing that the vault has fulfilled its purpose. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Svalbard Global Seed Vault stores more than one million seed samples from almost every country.
  2. In 2015, Syrian researchers withdrew seeds from the Svalbard vault to replace a seed bank damaged by war, just as the vault was designed to allow.
  3. The Svalbard Global Seed Vault is inside a mountain on an Arctic island in Norway.
  4. The vault's purpose is to protect crop diversity against disasters, wars and climate change.
Answer B. The goal is an example of the vault doing its job. Only the Syrian withdrawal after a war-damaged seed bank shows that happening; the others describe the vault or its purpose without an example.
mock-1-rw2h-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Rosalind Franklin was a British chemist.
• In 1952, she produced Photo 51, an X-ray image of DNA.
• The image was key evidence for DNA's double-helix structure.
• James Watson and Francis Crick published the double-helix model in 1953 after seeing her image, without her knowledge.
The student wants to emphasise Franklin's contribution to the discovery of DNA's structure. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Rosalind Franklin was a British chemist who worked with X-rays.
  2. Watson and Crick published the double-helix model of DNA in 1953.
  3. Franklin's 1952 X-ray image, Photo 51, provided key evidence for the double-helix structure that Watson and Crick published in 1953.
  4. DNA has a double-helix structure, as shown by research in the 1950s.
Answer C. Only the sentence centred on Franklin's image as "key evidence" for the model highlights her contribution. The others leave her out or do not link her to the discovery.

Math · Module 1 · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-1-m1-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $4x + 9 = 33$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $3$
  2. $4$
  3. $5$
  4. $6$
Answer D. Subtract $9$: $4x = 24$, so $x = 6$.
mock-1-m1-02 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 3x - 5$. What is the value of $f(4)$?
  1. $-2$
  2. $7$
  3. $12$
  4. $17$
Answer B. $f(4) = 3(4) - 5 = 12 - 5 = 7$.
mock-1-m1-03 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
What is $30\%$ of $250$?
  1. $75$
  2. $83$
  3. $175$
  4. $220$
Answer A. $0.30 \times 250 = 75$.
mock-1-m1-04 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 1
The line $y = 2x + b$ passes through the point $(3, 11)$. What is the value of $b$?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. Substitute: $11 = 2(3) + b = 6 + b$, so $b = 5$.
mock-1-m1-05 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 1
Two angles of a triangle measure $48^\circ$ and $67^\circ$. What is the measure of the third angle?
  1. $65^\circ$
  2. $75^\circ$
  3. $115^\circ$
  4. $133^\circ$
Answer A. The angles of a triangle add to $180^\circ$: $180 - 48 - 67 = 65$.
mock-1-m1-06 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $x^2 - 49$?
  1. $(x - 7)^2$
  2. $(x + 7)^2$
  3. $(x - 7)(x + 7)$
  4. $(x - 49)(x + 1)$
Answer C. $x^2 - 49$ is a difference of two squares: $x^2 - 7^2 = (x - 7)(x + 7)$.
mock-1-m1-07 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A car travels $210$ miles on $7$ gallons of fuel. At this rate, how many gallons are needed to travel $330$ miles?
  1. $9$
  2. $10$
  3. $11$
  4. $12$
Answer C. The car does $210 \div 7 = 30$ miles per gallon, so $330$ miles needs $330 \div 30 = 11$ gallons.
mock-1-m1-08 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$3x + 2y = 16$
$x + 2y = 8$


What is the value of $x$ in the solution to the system of equations above?
  1. $2$
  2. $3$
  3. $4$
  4. $5$
Answer C. Subtract the second equation from the first: $2x = 8$, so $x = 4$.
mock-1-m1-09 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
What is the positive solution to $x^2 = 5x + 24$?

Type-in answer: 8

Answer 8. Rearrange: $x^2 - 5x - 24 = 0$, which factors as $(x - 8)(x + 3) = 0$. The positive solution is $x = 8$.
mock-1-m1-10 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
Which value of $x$ satisfies the inequality $5 - 2x > 11$?
  1. $-4$
  2. $-3$
  3. $0$
  4. $3$
Answer A. $5 - 2x > 11$ gives $-2x > 6$, so $x < -3$ (dividing by a negative flips the sign). Only $-4$ is less than $-3$.
mock-1-m1-11 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 2
The mean of five numbers is $12$. Four of the numbers are $8$, $10$, $14$ and $15$. What is the fifth number?
  1. $11$
  2. $12$
  3. $13$
  4. $14$
Answer C. The five numbers total $5 \times 12 = 60$. The four given add to $47$, so the fifth is $60 - 47 = 13$.
mock-1-m1-12 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $g$ is defined by $g(x) = x^2 - 6x + 10$. What is the minimum value of $g(x)$?
  1. $1$
  2. $3$
  3. $6$
  4. $10$
Answer A. Complete the square: $g(x) = (x - 3)^2 + 1$. The square is never negative, so the minimum is $1$, at $x = 3$.
mock-1-m1-13 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 2
A rectangular box has length $6$ cm, width $4$ cm and volume $96$ cubic centimetres. What is its height, in centimetres?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. Volume $= \ell w h$, so $96 = 6 \times 4 \times h = 24h$, giving $h = 4$.
mock-1-m1-14 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 2
A plumber charges a call-out fee plus an hourly rate. A $2$-hour job costs $\$130$ and a $5$-hour job costs $\$265$. What is the call-out fee?
  1. $\$25$
  2. $\$40$
  3. $\$45$
  4. $\$65$
Answer B. The extra $3$ hours cost $265 - 130 = 135$ dollars, so the rate is $\$45$ an hour. Then $130 = 40 + 2(45)$, so the fee is $\$40$.
mock-1-m1-15 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 2
A bag contains $4$ red, $6$ blue and $10$ green marbles. If one marble is chosen at random, what is the probability that it is not green?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{5}$
  2. $\dfrac{3}{10}$
  3. $\dfrac{1}{2}$
  4. $\dfrac{3}{5}$
Answer C. $10$ of the $20$ marbles are not green, so the probability is $\frac{10}{20} = \frac{1}{2}$.
mock-1-m1-16 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
If $\dfrac{x^2 + 5x + 6}{x + 2} = ax + b$ for all $x \ne -2$, where $a$ and $b$ are constants, what is the value of $a + b$?
  1. $3$
  2. $4$
  3. $5$
  4. $6$
Answer B. $x^2 + 5x + 6 = (x + 2)(x + 3)$, so the expression equals $x + 3$. Then $a = 1$, $b = 3$ and $a + b = 4$.
mock-1-m1-17 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 3
The price of a phone rose by $20\%$ and was later reduced by $25\%$. The final price was $\$450$. What was the original price, in dollars?

Type-in answer: 500

Answer 500. Final $=$ original $\times 1.2 \times 0.75 =$ original $\times 0.9$. So the original was $450 \div 0.9 = 500$ dollars.
mock-1-m1-18 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 3
In right triangle $ABC$, angle $C$ is the right angle, $AC = 8$ and $BC = 15$. What is $\sin A$?
  1. $\dfrac{8}{17}$
  2. $\dfrac{8}{15}$
  3. $\dfrac{15}{17}$
  4. $\dfrac{17}{15}$
Answer C. The hypotenuse is $AB = \sqrt{8^2 + 15^2} = 17$. The side opposite angle $A$ is $BC = 15$, so $\sin A = \frac{15}{17}$.
mock-1-m1-19 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 3
$y = 3x - 2$
$6x - 2y = k$


For what value of $k$ does the system of equations above have infinitely many solutions?
  1. $-4$
  2. $-2$
  3. $2$
  4. $4$
Answer D. Substitute $y = 3x - 2$: $6x - 2(3x - 2) = 4$, so the second equation is the same line when $k = 4$.
mock-1-m1-20 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
A population of bacteria doubles every $3$ hours. There are $500$ bacteria now. Which function gives the number of bacteria $t$ hours from now?
  1. $P(t) = 500(2)^{3t}$
  2. $P(t) = 500(3)^{t/2}$
  3. $P(t) = 500 + 2t$
  4. $P(t) = 500(2)^{t/3}$
Answer D. Doubling every $3$ hours means the exponent counts $3$-hour periods: $t/3$. So $P(t) = 500(2)^{t/3}$.
mock-1-m1-21 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle has equation $(x - 4)^2 + (y + 1)^2 = 36$. What is the circumference of the circle? (Use $\pi \approx 3.14$ and round to the nearest whole number.)

Type-in answer: 38

Answer 38. The radius is $\sqrt{36} = 6$, so the circumference is $2\pi(6) = 12\pi \approx 37.68$, which rounds to $38$.
mock-1-m1-22 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 3
A line of best fit for a scatterplot of weekly study hours $h$ and test score $s$ is $s = 4.5h + 52$. Which statement is the best interpretation of the number $4.5$?
  1. The predicted test score for a student who studies 0 hours a week
  2. The predicted increase in test score for each additional weekly study hour
  3. The number of hours a student must study to score 52
  4. The highest test score in the data
Answer B. $4.5$ is the slope: the change in predicted score for each one-unit increase in $h$, i.e. each extra weekly hour. $52$ is the predicted score at $0$ hours.

Math · Module 2 (easier) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-1-m2e-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $x - 12 = 7$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $-19$
  2. $-5$
  3. $5$
  4. $19$
Answer D. Add $12$ to both sides: $x = 19$.
mock-1-m2e-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 1
A recipe uses $3$ cups of flour for every $2$ cups of milk. How many cups of flour are needed for $8$ cups of milk?
  1. $6$
  2. $10$
  3. $12$
  4. $16$
Answer C. $8$ cups of milk is $4$ times $2$, so use $4 \times 3 = 12$ cups of flour.
mock-1-m2e-03 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
Which equation represents a line with slope $4$ that passes through $(0, -3)$?
  1. $y = -3x + 4$
  2. $y = 3x - 4$
  3. $y = 4x + 3$
  4. $y = 4x - 3$
Answer D. In $y = mx + b$, $m$ is the slope and $b$ the $y$-intercept: $m = 4$, $b = -3$.
mock-1-m2e-04 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $2(x + 3) = 20$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 7

Answer 7. Divide by $2$: $x + 3 = 10$, so $x = 7$.
mock-1-m2e-05 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 1
What is the area of a rectangle with length $9$ and width $4$?
  1. $13$
  2. $26$
  3. $36$
  4. $45$
Answer C. Area $= 9 \times 4 = 36$.
mock-1-m2e-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
A class has $25$ students, and $40\%$ of them walk to school. How many students walk to school?
  1. $8$
  2. $10$
  3. $12$
  4. $15$
Answer B. $0.4 \times 25 = 10$.
mock-1-m2e-07 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 1
Which expression is equivalent to $3(x + 4) - x$?
  1. $2x + 4$
  2. $2x + 12$
  3. $3x + 12$
  4. $4x + 12$
Answer B. $3x + 12 - x = 2x + 12$.
mock-1-m2e-08 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 1
What is the median of the data set $4, 9, 2, 7, 5$?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. In order: $2, 4, 5, 7, 9$. The middle value is $5$.
mock-1-m2e-09 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$x + y = 10$
$x - y = 4$


What is the value of $y$?
  1. $3$
  2. $4$
  3. $6$
  4. $7$
Answer A. Add the equations: $2x = 14$, so $x = 7$ and $y = 10 - 7 = 3$.
mock-1-m2e-10 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
If $h(x) = x^2 + 1$, what is the value of $h(-3)$?
  1. $-8$
  2. $-5$
  3. $7$
  4. $10$
Answer D. $(-3)^2 + 1 = 9 + 1 = 10$.
mock-1-m2e-11 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
A student has $\$40$ to spend on notebooks that cost $\$3$ each. Which inequality gives the possible numbers $n$ of notebooks the student can buy?
  1. $3n \le 40$
  2. $3n \ge 40$
  3. $n + 3 \le 40$
  4. $40n \le 3$
Answer A. $n$ notebooks cost $3n$ dollars, which must be at most $40$: $3n \le 40$.
mock-1-m2e-12 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 2
Two lines intersect, forming an angle of $112^\circ$. What is the measure of each of the two angles next to it?
  1. $58^\circ$
  2. $68^\circ$
  3. $78^\circ$
  4. $112^\circ$
Answer B. Angles next to each other on a straight line add to $180^\circ$: $180 - 112 = 68$.
mock-1-m2e-13 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 2
What is the slope of the line that passes through $(1, 2)$ and $(5, 14)$?

Type-in answer: 3

Answer 3. Slope $= \frac{14 - 2}{5 - 1} = \frac{12}{4} = 3$.
mock-1-m2e-14 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 2
A fair six-sided number cube is rolled once. What is the probability of rolling a number less than $3$?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{6}$
  2. $\dfrac{1}{3}$
  3. $\dfrac{1}{2}$
  4. $\dfrac{2}{3}$
Answer B. Only $1$ and $2$ are less than $3$: $\frac{2}{6} = \frac{1}{3}$.
mock-1-m2e-15 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
What are the solutions to $x^2 - 9x + 20 = 0$?
  1. $x = -5$ and $x = -4$
  2. $x = -5$ and $x = 4$
  3. $x = 4$ and $x = 5$
  4. $x = 2$ and $x = 10$
Answer C. Factor: $(x - 4)(x - 5) = 0$, so $x = 4$ or $x = 5$.
mock-1-m2e-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A runner completes $5$ kilometres in $25$ minutes. At the same pace, how long does $8$ kilometres take?
  1. $32$ minutes
  2. $36$ minutes
  3. $40$ minutes
  4. $45$ minutes
Answer C. The pace is $5$ minutes per kilometre, so $8$ km takes $40$ minutes.
mock-1-m2e-17 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
A ladder $13$ feet long leans against a wall. The foot of the ladder is $5$ feet from the wall. How high up the wall does the ladder reach, in feet?

Type-in answer: 12

Answer 12. By Pythagoras, height $= \sqrt{13^2 - 5^2} = \sqrt{144} = 12$.
mock-1-m2e-18 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $(x + 5)^2$?
  1. $x^2 + 25$
  2. $x^2 + 5x + 25$
  3. $x^2 + 10x + 25$
  4. $2x + 10$
Answer C. $(x + 5)(x + 5) = x^2 + 5x + 5x + 25 = x^2 + 10x + 25$.
mock-1-m2e-19 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 2
A random sample of $200$ voters in a town found that $58\%$ support a new park. Which conclusion is best supported?
  1. Exactly 58% of all the town's voters support the park.
  2. About 58% of the town's voters probably support the park.
  3. Most voters in every town support new parks.
  4. Exactly 116 voters in the town support the park.
Answer B. A random sample gives an estimate for the whole town, not an exact figure, and says nothing about other towns.
mock-1-m2e-20 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 2
A tank holds $500$ litres of water and drains at $20$ litres per minute. Which function gives the water $W$, in litres, left after $t$ minutes?
  1. $W = 500 - 20t$
  2. $W = 500 + 20t$
  3. $W = 20 - 500t$
  4. $W = 500t - 20$
Answer A. It starts at $500$ and loses $20$ each minute: $W = 500 - 20t$.
mock-1-m2e-21 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
A shirt costs $\$24$ after a $20\%$ discount. What was the price before the discount, in dollars?

Type-in answer: 30

Answer 30. The sale price is $80\%$ of the original: $0.8p = 24$, so $p = 30$.
mock-1-m2e-22 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 2
A circle has a diameter of $10$. What is its area?
  1. $10\pi$
  2. $20\pi$
  3. $25\pi$
  4. $100\pi$
Answer C. The radius is $5$, so the area is $\pi(5)^2 = 25\pi$.

Math · Module 2 (harder) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-1-m2h-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 2
If $\dfrac{3x - 4}{5} = \dfrac{x + 2}{3}$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $\dfrac{11}{2}$
  2. $\dfrac{13}{2}$
  3. $7$
  4. $11$
Answer A. Cross-multiply: $3(3x - 4) = 5(x + 2)$, so $9x - 12 = 5x + 10$, $4x = 22$ and $x = \frac{11}{2}$.
mock-1-m2h-02 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = (x - 2)(x + 6)$. What is the $x$-coordinate of the vertex of the graph of $y = f(x)$?
  1. $-4$
  2. $-2$
  3. $2$
  4. $4$
Answer B. The vertex lies midway between the zeros $x = 2$ and $x = -6$: $\frac{2 + (-6)}{2} = -2$.
mock-1-m2h-03 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$2x - 3y = -1$
$4x + y = 19$


If $(x, y)$ is the solution to the system of equations above, what is the value of $x + y$?

Type-in answer: 7

Answer 7. From the second equation, $y = 19 - 4x$. Substitute: $2x - 3(19 - 4x) = -1$, so $14x = 56$, $x = 4$ and $y = 3$. Then $x + y = 7$.
mock-1-m2h-04 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
A town's population grew from $40{,}000$ to $46{,}000$. By what percentage did the population increase?
  1. $6\%$
  2. $13\%$
  3. $15\%$
  4. $87\%$
Answer C. The increase is $6{,}000$, and $\frac{6{,}000}{40{,}000} = 0.15 = 15\%$.
mock-1-m2h-05 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
Which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{1}{x - 3} - \dfrac{1}{x + 3}$ for $x \ne \pm 3$?
  1. $\dfrac{6}{x^2 - 9}$
  2. $\dfrac{-6}{x^2 - 9}$
  3. $\dfrac{2x}{x^2 - 9}$
  4. $\dfrac{6}{x^2 + 9}$
Answer A. Common denominator $(x - 3)(x + 3) = x^2 - 9$: $\frac{(x + 3) - (x - 3)}{x^2 - 9} = \frac{6}{x^2 - 9}$.
mock-1-m2h-06 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
In a right triangle, one acute angle measures $x^\circ$ and $\cos x^\circ = \dfrac{12}{13}$. What is $\sin\left(90^\circ - x^\circ\right)$?
  1. $\dfrac{5}{13}$
  2. $\dfrac{12}{13}$
  3. $\dfrac{13}{12}$
  4. $\dfrac{5}{12}$
Answer B. For complementary angles, $\sin(90^\circ - x^\circ) = \cos x^\circ = \frac{12}{13}$.
mock-1-m2h-07 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
$y = x^2 - 4x + 7$
$y = 2x - 2$


The system of equations above has exactly one solution $(x, y)$. What is the value of $y$?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. Set them equal: $x^2 - 4x + 7 = 2x - 2$, so $x^2 - 6x + 9 = 0$, $(x - 3)^2 = 0$ and $x = 3$. Then $y = 2(3) - 2 = 4$.
mock-1-m2h-08 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 3
Data set A is $10, 12, 14, 16, 18$. Data set B is formed by adding $5$ to every value in A. Which statement is true?
  1. Both the mean and the standard deviation increase by 5.
  2. The mean is unchanged and the standard deviation increases by 5.
  3. Neither the mean nor the standard deviation changes.
  4. The mean increases by 5 and the standard deviation is unchanged.
Answer D. Adding the same number to every value shifts the centre by $5$ but does not change how spread out the values are.
mock-1-m2h-09 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 3
The graph of the linear function $f$ passes through $(2, 7)$ and $(6, -1)$. What is the $x$-intercept of the graph?
  1. $\left(\dfrac{11}{2}, 0\right)$
  2. $\left(\dfrac{9}{2}, 0\right)$
  3. $\left(\dfrac{7}{2}, 0\right)$
  4. $(11, 0)$
Answer A. Slope $= \frac{-1 - 7}{6 - 2} = -2$, so $f(x) = -2x + 11$. Setting $f(x) = 0$ gives $x = \frac{11}{2}$.
mock-1-m2h-10 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
A ball is thrown upward. Its height, in metres, after $t$ seconds is $h(t) = -5t^2 + 20t + 1$. What is the maximum height of the ball?
  1. $15$ metres
  2. $20$ metres
  3. $21$ metres
  4. $25$ metres
Answer C. The maximum is at $t = \frac{-20}{2(-5)} = 2$: $h(2) = -20 + 40 + 1 = 21$ metres.
mock-1-m2h-11 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 3
A map uses a scale of $1$ centimetre to $2.5$ kilometres. Two towns are $14$ centimetres apart on the map. A cyclist rides between them at $15$ kilometres per hour. How many minutes does the trip take?

Type-in answer: 140

Answer 140. Distance $= 14 \times 2.5 = 35$ km. Time $= \frac{35}{15}$ hours $= \frac{7}{3}$ hours $= 140$ minutes.
mock-1-m2h-12 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle has centre $O$ and radius $9$. Points $A$ and $B$ lie on the circle, and central angle $AOB$ measures $80^\circ$. What is the length of minor arc $AB$?
  1. $2\pi$
  2. $4\pi$
  3. $8\pi$
  4. $18\pi$
Answer B. Arc length $= \frac{80}{360} \times 2\pi(9) = \frac{2}{9} \times 18\pi = 4\pi$.
mock-1-m2h-13 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
A cinema recorded the snacks bought by $300$ customers.


PopcornNo popcornTotal
Drink12045165
No drink6075135
Total180120300



A customer who bought popcorn is chosen at random. What is the probability that this customer also bought a drink?
  1. $\dfrac{2}{5}$
  2. $\dfrac{8}{11}$
  3. $\dfrac{2}{3}$
  4. $\dfrac{11}{20}$
Answer C. Only the $180$ popcorn buyers count; $120$ of them bought a drink: $\frac{120}{180} = \frac{2}{3}$.
mock-1-m2h-14 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
If $2^{x + 3} = 4^{x - 1}$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. Write $4^{x - 1} = 2^{2x - 2}$. Then $x + 3 = 2x - 2$, so $x = 5$.
mock-1-m2h-15 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 3
A delivery van can carry at most $1{,}200$ kilograms. Boxes of type P weigh $40$ kg and boxes of type Q weigh $25$ kg. If the van carries $18$ boxes of type P, what is the greatest number of type Q boxes it can also carry?
  1. $18$
  2. $19$
  3. $20$
  4. $21$
Answer B. $18 \times 40 = 720$ kg, leaving $480$ kg. $25q \le 480$ gives $q \le 19.2$, so at most $19$ boxes.
mock-1-m2h-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Studies and experiments · difficulty 3
Researchers randomly assigned $300$ volunteers to either take a new vitamin or a placebo for six months. The vitamin group reported significantly fewer colds. Which conclusion is best supported?
  1. The vitamin prevents colds in everyone who takes it.
  2. People who choose to take vitamins catch fewer colds.
  3. The vitamin likely reduces colds among people similar to the volunteers.
  4. The study shows nothing, because volunteers were used.
Answer C. Random assignment supports a cause-and-effect conclusion, but only for people like the volunteers, and not for every individual.
mock-1-m2h-17 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A cylinder has radius $3$ and height $8$. A cone has the same radius and the same volume as the cylinder. What is the height of the cone?
  1. $8$
  2. $12$
  3. $16$
  4. $24$
Answer D. Cylinder: $\pi(3)^2(8) = 72\pi$. Cone: $\frac{1}{3}\pi(3)^2 h = 3\pi h$. Setting $3\pi h = 72\pi$ gives $h = 24$.
mock-1-m2h-18 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The function $p(x) = 800(0.9)^x$ models the value, in dollars, of a laptop $x$ years after purchase. Which statement is true?
  1. The value decreases by $90 each year.
  2. The value decreases by 90% each year.
  3. The value increases by 10% each year.
  4. The value decreases by 10% each year.
Answer D. Multiplying by $0.9$ each year keeps $90\%$ of the value, a $10\%$ decrease each year.
mock-1-m2h-19 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 3
In triangle $PQR$, $PQ = PR$ and angle $Q$ measures $52^\circ$. What is the measure of angle $P$, in degrees?

Type-in answer: 76

Answer 76. $PQ = PR$ makes the base angles at $Q$ and $R$ equal: each $52^\circ$. So angle $P = 180 - 104 = 76$.
mock-1-m2h-20 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 3
Line $\ell$ has equation $3x - 4y = 12$. Line $m$ is perpendicular to $\ell$ and passes through $(0, 5)$. Which equation represents line $m$?
  1. $y = -\dfrac{4}{3}x + 5$
  2. $y = -\dfrac{3}{4}x + 5$
  3. $y = \dfrac{3}{4}x + 5$
  4. $y = \dfrac{4}{3}x + 5$
Answer A. Line $\ell$ is $y = \frac{3}{4}x - 3$, slope $\frac{3}{4}$. A perpendicular slope is $-\frac{4}{3}$, and the $y$-intercept is $5$.
mock-1-m2h-21 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 3
A survey of a random sample of students estimates that $64\%$ of students at a school use the library weekly, with a margin of error of $5\%$. Which statement is best supported?
  1. Exactly 64% of all students use the library weekly.
  2. The true percentage is plausibly between 59% and 69%.
  3. At least 69% of students use the library weekly.
  4. The survey's results cannot be applied to the school.
Answer B. The margin of error gives a plausible range for the true value: $64\% \pm 5\%$, i.e. $59\%$ to $69\%$.
mock-1-m2h-22 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
How many real solutions does the equation $3x^2 - 2x + 5 = 0$ have?
  1. Zero
  2. Exactly one
  3. Exactly two
  4. Infinitely many
Answer A. The discriminant is $(-2)^2 - 4(3)(5) = 4 - 60 = -56$, which is negative, so there are no real solutions.

SAT Practice Test 2

Reading and Writing · Module 1 · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-2-rw1-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
After the long drought, the farmers were ______ to see dark clouds gathering over the hills, and many stood outside to watch the first drops fall.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. indifferent
  2. annoyed
  3. puzzled
  4. relieved
Answer D. After a long drought, rain is welcome, and people stood outside to watch it, so the farmers felt "relieved".
mock-2-rw1-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The museum guide gave a ______ explanation of the painting, covering its history, the artist's technique and the meaning of every symbol in it.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. thorough
  2. brief
  3. careless
  4. confusing
Answer A. Covering history, technique and every symbol is complete and detailed: "thorough". "Brief" and "careless" contradict this.
mock-2-rw1-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The new law was intended to ______ the process of starting a small business: applicants now fill in one online form instead of visiting four separate government offices.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. complicate
  2. simplify
  3. investigate
  4. postpone
Answer B. Replacing four office visits with one online form makes the process easier: the law was meant to "simplify" it.
mock-2-rw1-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Although the composer was celebrated in her lifetime, her music fell into ______ after her death, and it was not performed again for nearly a century until a researcher found her manuscripts in an attic.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. controversy
  2. fashion
  3. obscurity
  4. abundance
Answer C. Not being performed for nearly a century, until rediscovered in an attic, means the music became unknown: it fell into "obscurity".
mock-2-rw1-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 1
Every winter, monarch butterflies from across North America fly thousands of kilometres to the same small patch of forest in central Mexico. No single butterfly makes the round trip: the ones that return north in spring are the great-grandchildren of those that left the year before.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To explain how butterflies find their way using the sun
  2. To argue that the forests in Mexico should be protected
  3. To describe a remarkable feature of the monarch butterfly's migration
  4. To compare monarch butterflies with migrating birds
Answer C. The text describes the monarchs' long journey to one forest and the surprising fact that no single butterfly completes the round trip. It makes no argument and no comparison.
mock-2-rw1-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Many people assume that the Great Wall of China is a single continuous wall. In fact, it is a series of walls, built and rebuilt by different dynasties over roughly two thousand years. Some sections are made of stone and brick, while older ones are made of packed earth.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It argues that the Great Wall should be rebuilt.
  2. It tells the story of a single dynasty that built the wall.
  3. It compares the Great Wall with other famous walls.
  4. It corrects a common belief and then gives details that support the correction.
Answer D. The first sentence gives a belief; "In fact" corrects it (a series of walls), and the last sentence adds supporting detail about different materials.
mock-2-rw1-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
The following text is from a 1911 novel. The narrator has just moved to a small coastal town.


At first the town seemed to me nothing but grey stone and wind. But by the end of my first month I had learned which baker saved the warm loaves for the fishermen, which path along the cliff smelled of wild thyme in the evenings, and which neighbour would lend a lamp without being asked. The stone and the wind were still there; I had simply stopped noticing them.
Which choice best describes the function of the last sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It shows that the narrator's view of the town has changed although the town itself has not.
  2. It explains why the narrator decided to leave the town.
  3. It describes the weather the narrator experienced in the first month.
  4. It suggests that the narrator's neighbours were unfriendly.
Answer A. The town is first described as "grey stone and wind", then through warm details the narrator learns. The last sentence says the stone and wind "were still there" but the narrator stopped noticing them, so the narrator changed, not the town.
mock-2-rw1-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
Some urban planners argue that cities should replace parking spaces with bike lanes and wider pavements. Fewer parking spaces, they claim, encourage people to walk, cycle or take public transport, which reduces traffic and pollution.


Text 2
When one city removed street parking in its centre, shop owners worried they would lose customers. Sales data from the following year showed that most shops had more customers than before, largely because more people were walking and cycling past them.
Based on the texts, how would the planners in Text 1 most likely respond to the results described in Text 2?
  1. They would argue that the shop owners' worries were justified.
  2. They would see the results as supporting their view that removing parking changes how people travel.
  3. They would claim that the results show bike lanes increase pollution.
  4. They would say that the results are unrelated to their argument.
Answer B. Text 2 found more people walking and cycling after parking was removed, which is exactly what the planners in Text 1 predicted. The shop owners' worries turned out not to be justified.
mock-2-rw1-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
Earthworms are often called "ecosystem engineers." As they tunnel, they mix the soil and create channels that let air and water reach plant roots. They also eat dead leaves and turn them into nutrient-rich droppings that help plants grow.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Earthworms eat only dead leaves.
  2. Earthworms improve soil in ways that help plants.
  3. Engineers have studied earthworm tunnels to design buildings.
  4. Plants cannot grow in soil that contains earthworms.
Answer B. Each detail (mixing soil, letting air and water in, turning leaves into nutrients) shows earthworms improving soil for plants, which is why they are called "ecosystem engineers".
mock-2-rw1-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from a 1902 short story.


Old Jonah had kept the lighthouse for forty years. Ships no longer needed him: a new electric beacon on the headland did his work automatically. Still, every evening at dusk, he climbed the hundred and twelve steps, polished the great lens and lit the lamp, as if the sea might be waiting for him.
According to the text, why does Jonah light the lamp each evening?
  1. Because ships have complained that the coast is too dark
  2. Because the new electric beacon has stopped working
  3. Out of long habit and loyalty, even though his work is no longer needed
  4. Because he has been ordered to keep the lighthouse running
Answer C. Ships "no longer needed him", yet he lights the lamp "as if the sea might be waiting", after forty years, which points to habit and loyalty. Nothing says the beacon failed or that he was ordered to.
mock-2-rw1-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
A nutrition researcher claims that eating meals at regular times each day helps people sleep better.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support the researcher's claim?
  1. Some foods contain substances that make people feel sleepy.
  2. Many people eat breakfast at the same time every day.
  3. People who sleep well tend to exercise regularly.
  4. Participants randomly assigned to eat at fixed times for a month fell asleep faster than participants who ate at varying times.
Answer D. The claim links regular meal times to better sleep. Randomly assigning meal schedules and finding faster sleep with fixed times supports that link directly.
mock-2-rw1-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Art historian Lena Fischer argues that a painting long credited to a famous Dutch master was actually painted by one of his students, who copied the master's style closely.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Fischer's argument?
  1. Chemical analysis shows that the painting's blue pigment was not available until ten years after the master's death.
  2. The painting is displayed next to several of the master's best-known works.
  3. The master is known to have taught many students in his workshop.
  4. Critics have praised the painting as one of the master's finest.
Answer A. A pigment that did not exist until after the master died means he could not have painted it, which supports the claim that someone else did. That the master had students does not show this painting is by one.
mock-2-rw1-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 1
A café recorded the drinks it sold on one Saturday.


DrinkCups sold
Tea85
Coffee140
Hot chocolate60
Juice45



On that Saturday, the café sold more cups of ______ than of any other drink.
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to complete the statement?
  1. juice
  2. tea
  3. hot chocolate
  4. coffee
Answer D. Coffee has the highest number in the table: 140 cups.
mock-2-rw1-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
A conservation group counted nesting pairs of two seabird species on an island.


YearSpecies XSpecies Y
2010420310
2014380360
2018330415
2022290470



A biologist suggests that the two species may be competing for the same nesting sites, so that as one species gains sites, the other loses them.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the biologist's suggestion?
  1. From 2010 to 2022, Species X's nesting pairs fell steadily while Species Y's rose steadily.
  2. Species Y had more nesting pairs than Species X in 2022.
  3. Species X had 420 nesting pairs in 2010.
  4. The total number of nesting pairs was about the same in 2010 and 2022.
Answer A. The suggestion is that one species gains as the other loses. Only the opposite trends across all four counts (X falling while Y rises) show that pattern.
mock-2-rw1-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
Desert tortoises spend most of the hottest part of the year in burrows they dig underground, coming out mainly in the cooler early morning. Burrow temperatures stay far lower than temperatures at the surface during summer afternoons. This suggests that burrows help tortoises ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. find more food than they could at the surface.
  2. avoid the extreme heat of the desert surface.
  3. stay warm during cold summer nights.
  4. hide their eggs from other tortoises.
Answer B. Tortoises stay in burrows during the hottest time, and burrows are much cooler than the surface, so the burrows help them escape the heat.
mock-2-rw1-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
In a study of two groups of children learning to read, one group practised with books that matched each child's interests, while the other used a standard set of books. After six months, the two groups scored equally well on reading tests, but children in the first group read more books in their free time. If the study's results hold more widely, matching books to children's interests may ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. make children less interested in reading in their free time.
  2. raise reading test scores more than standard books do.
  3. encourage children to read more for pleasure, even if it does not raise test scores.
  4. be the only way to teach children to read.
Answer C. Test scores were equal, but the interest-matched group read more in their free time. So the benefit is more reading for pleasure, not higher scores.
mock-2-rw1-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 1
The festival includes music, food stalls and a ______ it ends each night with fireworks over the river.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. parade;
  2. parade,
  3. parade
  4. parade and,
Answer A. Both parts are complete sentences, so a semicolon joins them. A comma alone would be a comma splice.
mock-2-rw1-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The scientists collected three types of ______ ice cores, ocean sediments and tree rings.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. samples;
  2. samples:
  3. samples,
  4. samples
Answer B. A complete sentence introduces a list, so a colon is used. A semicolon would need a complete sentence after it.
mock-2-rw1-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
Frida Kahlo, the Mexican painter known for her vivid self-______ often included plants and animals from her garden in her work.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. portraits;
  2. portraits
  3. portraits,
  4. portraits:
Answer C. The phrase "the Mexican painter known for her vivid self-portraits" renames Frida Kahlo and opens with a comma, so it must close with one before the verb "included".
mock-2-rw1-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The team had prepared for every ______ even so, the sudden power cut caught them by surprise.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. possibility, and
  2. possibility,
  3. possibility
  4. possibility;
Answer D. "Even so" starts a new complete sentence, so a semicolon is needed before it. A comma would create a comma splice, and "and" would clash with "even so".
mock-2-rw1-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 1
The books on the top shelf ______ older than the ones below.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. is
  2. has been
  3. was
  4. are
Answer D. The subject is "books", which is plural ("on the top shelf" describes them), so the plural "are" is needed.
mock-2-rw1-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
If the weather ______ clear tomorrow, the astronomy club will set up its telescopes in the park.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. stays
  2. stayed
  3. had stayed
  4. will have stayed
Answer A. In a sentence about the future with "will" in the main clause, the "if" clause uses the present tense: "stays".
mock-2-rw1-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Each of the paintings, along with the sketches that the artist made for them, ______ for the first time at the gallery this spring.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. are being shown
  2. is being shown
  3. have been shown
  4. were shown
Answer B. The subject is "Each", which is singular; "along with the sketches..." does not change that. "Is being shown" is singular and fits "this spring".
mock-2-rw1-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 1
Sloths move very slowly through the trees. ______, they are surprisingly good swimmers.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. For example
  2. Therefore
  3. However
  4. Likewise
Answer C. Being good swimmers contrasts with moving slowly in trees, so a contrast word fits.
mock-2-rw1-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
The bridge was closed for repairs for six months. ______, drivers had to take a route that added twenty minutes to their journey.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Above all
  2. In contrast
  3. For instance
  4. During that time
Answer D. The second sentence describes what happened while the bridge was closed, so a time transition fits.
mock-2-rw1-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Chess originated in India around the 6th century.
• The early version was called chaturanga.
• It spread to Persia, then to Europe.
• Modern rules were settled in Europe in the 15th century.
The student wants to describe how chess spread from its place of origin. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Chess began in India as chaturanga around the 6th century and spread to Persia and then Europe.
  2. Modern chess rules were settled in Europe in the 15th century.
  3. Chess is a very old game played around the world.
  4. The early version of chess was called chaturanga.
Answer A. Only the sentence tracing the route from India through Persia to Europe describes how chess spread.
mock-2-rw1-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Wangari Maathai was a Kenyan environmentalist.
• In 1977 she founded the Green Belt Movement.
• The movement has planted over 50 million trees.
• It paid women to plant trees, giving them income and fighting deforestation.
• Maathai won the Nobel Peace Prize in 2004.
The student wants to explain how the Green Belt Movement helped both people and the environment. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Wangari Maathai, a Kenyan environmentalist, won the Nobel Peace Prize in 2004.
  2. By paying women to plant trees, the Green Belt Movement gave them an income while fighting deforestation.
  3. The Green Belt Movement, founded in 1977, has planted over 50 million trees.
  4. Wangari Maathai founded the Green Belt Movement in Kenya.
Answer B. The goal is benefits to both people and the environment. Only the sentence linking women's income to fighting deforestation covers both.

Reading and Writing · Module 2 (easier) · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-2-rw2e-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The kitten was so ______ that it explored every corner of the new house within an hour.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. frightened
  2. sleepy
  3. curious
  4. lazy
Answer C. Exploring every corner quickly shows a strong wish to find out about things: "curious".
mock-2-rw2e-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The old map was too ______ to read clearly: the ink had faded and the paper was torn in several places.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. modern
  2. colourful
  3. accurate
  4. damaged
Answer D. Faded ink and torn paper describe a map in poor condition: "damaged".
mock-2-rw2e-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The coach praised the team's ______ effort: even when they were losing by ten points, no player stopped trying.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. persistent
  2. careless
  3. brief
  4. uneven
Answer A. Never stopping trying while losing shows effort that continues: "persistent".
mock-2-rw2e-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
Scientists hope the new vaccine will ______ the spread of the disease by protecting the people most likely to catch it.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. celebrate
  2. limit
  3. measure
  4. describe
Answer B. Protecting those most likely to catch the disease would reduce its spread, so the vaccine is meant to "limit" it.
mock-2-rw2e-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 1
Many lighthouses once had keepers who lived on site and lit the lamps each night. Today, most lighthouses are automated, with electric lights that switch on by themselves at dusk.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It explains how to become a lighthouse keeper.
  2. It contrasts how lighthouses worked in the past with how most work today.
  3. It describes one famous lighthouse in detail.
  4. It argues that lighthouses are no longer needed.
Answer B. The first sentence describes the past (keepers) and the second describes today (automation), a then-and-now contrast.
mock-2-rw2e-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Some of the world's oldest trees are bristlecone pines in the mountains of California. One tree, known as Methuselah, is more than 4,800 years old. The trees grow very slowly in cold, dry, rocky soil, and scientists think their slow growth is part of why they live so long.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To explain how scientists measure the age of trees
  2. To argue that bristlecone pines should be planted in cities
  3. To describe very old trees and suggest a reason for their long lives
  4. To compare California's trees with trees in other places
Answer C. The text describes bristlecone pines and Methuselah's age, then gives scientists' explanation (slow growth) for their long lives.
mock-2-rw2e-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Most bats find their way in the dark using echolocation: they make high-pitched sounds and listen for the echoes. Fruit bats, however, rely mainly on their large eyes and strong sense of smell to find the fruit they eat.
Which choice best describes the function of the second sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It argues that fruit bats have the best eyesight of any animal.
  2. It explains how echolocation works.
  3. It gives a second example of bats that use echolocation.
  4. It describes a group of bats that differs from the general pattern in the first sentence.
Answer D. "However" signals a contrast: most bats use echolocation, but fruit bats rely on sight and smell instead.
mock-2-rw2e-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Some people believe that goldfish have a memory of only three seconds.


Text 2
In experiments, goldfish learned to push a lever to get food and still remembered how to do it months later. Researchers have also trained goldfish to respond to particular sounds at feeding time.
Based on the texts, how would the researchers in Text 2 most likely respond to the belief in Text 1?
  1. They would say the belief is wrong, since goldfish can remember things for months.
  2. They would agree that goldfish forget things after a few seconds.
  3. They would say goldfish remember sounds but not actions.
  4. They would argue that goldfish should not be kept as pets.
Answer A. Goldfish remembering a lever task for months shows they remember far longer than three seconds, so the researchers would reject the belief.
mock-2-rw2e-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
Volunteers in a coastal town meet every month to clean the beach. They collect plastic bottles, fishing nets and other rubbish, and record what they find. The records help the town see where the rubbish comes from and how to stop it.

  1. Fishing nets are the most common type of rubbish on beaches.
  2. Volunteers clean a beach and keep records that help the town tackle rubbish.
  3. The town pays people to clean its beach every day.
  4. Plastic bottles cannot be recycled.
Answer B. The text describes volunteers cleaning the beach and recording what they find, and how those records help the town. The other choices are not stated.
mock-2-rw2e-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2

According to the text, how did Kenji improve his cooking?
  1. By cooking only dishes that were easy
  2. By taking a cooking class every Sunday
  3. By noting his mistakes and trying the recipes again
  4. By asking his grandmother to watch him cook
Answer C. The text says he "kept notes on what went wrong and tried again the next week". No class, easy dishes or supervision is mentioned.
mock-2-rw2e-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 1
A student claims that the school's new water fountains have led students to drink more water.

  1. The old fountains were installed many years ago.
  2. The new water fountains are a bright blue colour.
  3. Some students bring their own water bottles to school.
  4. The number of times the fountains were used each day doubled compared with the old ones, while sales of bottled drinks fell.
Answer D. Much more fountain use, together with fewer bottled drinks sold, suggests students are drinking more water from the fountains. Colour and age say nothing about drinking.

A gardener claims that planting marigolds next to tomato plants helps protect the tomatoes from insects.

  3. Tomatoes need plenty of sunlight to ripen.
  4. Many gardeners like the colour of marigolds.
Answer A. Fewer insect-damaged leaves on tomatoes next to marigolds, compared with tomatoes without them, directly supports the claim.

mock-2-rw2e-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 1
Squirrels bury thousands of nuts each autumn to eat in winter, but they do not find all of them. Some of the forgotten nuts sprout in spring. This suggests that squirrels may help ______

  1. nuts to stay fresh.
  2. new trees to grow.
  3. birds to find food.
  4. winter to end sooner.

mock-2-rw2e-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
A bakery started selling a smaller, cheaper version of its most popular cake. Afterwards, sales of the full-size cake stayed the same, but the bakery sold many of the smaller cakes to people who had never bought cake there before. This suggests that the smaller cake ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. was more popular with the bakery's regular customers.
  2. replaced the full-size cake as the bakery's main product.
  3. attracted new customers without reducing sales of the full-size cake.
  4. cost more to make than the full-size cake.
Answer C. Full-size sales did not change, and the small cakes went to people who had never bought cake there, so they drew in new customers.

mock-2-rw2e-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2

  2. The Statue of Liberty was a gift from France.

Reading and Writing · Module 2 (harder) · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-2-rw2h-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The historian's account of the trade route is ______: drawing on ship records, merchants' letters and customs ledgers in four languages, it traces the movement of goods across three centuries in remarkable detail.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. cursory
  2. speculative
  3. derivative
  4. comprehensive
Answer D. Using many kinds of sources across centuries "in remarkable detail" makes the account complete: "comprehensive". "Cursory" (hasty) and "speculative" (based on guesses) contradict this.
mock-2-rw2h-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The negotiator's tone was ______ throughout the talks; even when the other side made provocative demands, she responded calmly and kept the discussion focused on practical details.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. measured
  2. strident
  3. evasive
  4. capricious
Answer A. Responding calmly and staying practical under provocation describes a controlled, "measured" tone. "Strident" (harsh) and "capricious" (unpredictable) are the opposite.
mock-2-rw2h-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Early critics thought the poet's habit of leaving lines unfinished was careless, but many scholars now see it as ______: a deliberate technique that invites readers to complete the thought themselves.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. accidental
  2. intentional
  3. outdated
  4. imitative
Answer B. The colon explains the blank: "a deliberate technique". So scholars see the unfinished lines as "intentional", in contrast with the early critics' view of carelessness.
mock-2-rw2h-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The company's claims about its product's environmental benefits proved ______; independent testing found that the product produced nearly as much waste as the competitors it claimed to outperform.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. verifiable
  2. modest
  3. specious
  4. prescient
Answer C. Claims that testing showed to be untrue, though they seemed convincing, are "specious". "Verifiable" and "prescient" (showing foresight) contradict the testing.
mock-2-rw2h-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1923 poem.


I kept the letters in a cedar box
And did not read them for a dozen years;
Today I turned the small and rusted locks,
Expecting grief, and found instead no tears,
But only gladness that such words were said,
And gratitude for every line I read.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. The speaker describes writing letters and explains why they were never sent.
  2. The speaker describes avoiding something for years, then reports an unexpected reaction on finally facing it.
  3. The speaker compares two boxes of letters from different times.
  4. The speaker regrets keeping the letters and decides to destroy them.
Answer B. The speaker left the letters unread for twelve years, then opened them "expecting grief" but felt gladness and gratitude instead: avoidance followed by a surprising response.
mock-2-rw2h-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Conventional accounts treat the Industrial Revolution as a story of inventors and their machines. Economic historian Priya Natarajan argues that this emphasis is misleading. Many of the key machines, she notes, had been invented decades before they were widely used; what changed was the availability of cheap coal and growing markets that made adopting them profitable.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To show that inventors played no part in the Industrial Revolution
  2. To describe how the first steam engines were built
  3. To present an argument that economic conditions, rather than inventions alone, drove industrial change
  4. To compare coal production in different countries
Answer C. The text introduces Natarajan's view that the usual focus on inventors misleads: machines existed for decades, and cheap coal and markets made using them pay. She qualifies the inventors' role but does not say they played no part.
mock-2-rw2h-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Octopuses can change the colour and texture of their skin in a fraction of a second, matching rocks, coral or sand. Strangely, most octopuses appear to be colour-blind: their eyes have only one type of light receptor. Researchers have proposed that light-sensitive proteins in the skin itself may help octopuses sense the colours around them.
Which choice best describes the function of the second sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It contradicts the researchers mentioned in the third sentence.
  2. It provides evidence that octopuses cannot change colour.
  3. It explains how octopuses match their surroundings so quickly.
  4. It presents a puzzling fact that the third sentence offers a possible explanation for.
Answer D. Colour-blind animals matching colours is puzzling ("Strangely"); the third sentence proposes how they might sense colour anyway. The second sentence does not deny that octopuses change colour.
mock-2-rw2h-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
Some educators argue that handwriting lessons are outdated now that most writing is done on keyboards. Time spent practising handwriting, they say, would be better used teaching typing and digital skills.


Text 2
In a study of young children, those who practised writing letters by hand later recognised those letters more quickly than children who practised typing them. The researchers suggest that forming letters by hand helps children learn their shapes, which supports early reading.
Based on the texts, how would the researchers in Text 2 most likely respond to the educators in Text 1?
  1. By cautioning that handwriting practice may have benefits for young children's reading that typing does not provide
  2. By agreeing that handwriting lessons should be replaced by typing lessons
  3. By arguing that typing harms children's ability to read
  4. By claiming that most writing is still done by hand
Answer A. The study found handwriting helped children recognise letters, which supports reading. The researchers would warn that dropping handwriting could lose this benefit. They do not claim typing does harm.
mock-2-rw2h-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from Charlotte Brontë's 1847 novel Jane Eyre.


I was glad of it: I never liked long walks, especially on chilly afternoons: dreadful to me was the coming home in the raw twilight, with nipped fingers and toes, and a heart saddened by the chidings of Bessie, the nurse.
Based on the text, why is the narrator glad?
  1. Because she will not have to go on a long walk, which she disliked
  2. Because she enjoys chilly afternoons outdoors
  3. Because Bessie has praised her
  4. Because she has finally reached home
Answer A. She is glad of "it" and immediately explains that she "never liked long walks", especially coming home cold and scolded. So she is glad to avoid a walk.
mock-2-rw2h-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 3
Researchers studying city trees found that streets with more trees had lower summer temperatures, fewer reports of heat-related illness and higher property values. However, trees were far more common in wealthier neighbourhoods. The researchers argue that planting trees in poorer neighbourhoods could reduce health risks for the people most exposed to extreme heat.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Wealthy neighbourhoods should remove some of their trees.
  2. Because trees bring benefits that are unevenly spread, planting more in poorer areas could improve residents' health.
  3. Property values rise whenever trees are planted.
  4. Extreme heat affects all neighbourhoods equally.
Answer B. The text links trees to cooler streets and better health, notes that trees are concentrated in wealthier areas, and concludes that planting in poorer areas could reduce health risks. Nothing suggests removing trees.
mock-2-rw2h-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1915 novel. Clara is about to give her first public speech.


Clara had rehearsed the speech so many times that she could have recited it in her sleep. Yet as she stepped onto the platform, her hands trembled so badly that the pages rattled, and for a terrible moment the first line would not come.
Which quotation from the text most effectively illustrates that Clara is nervous?
  1. "she could have recited it in her sleep"
  2. "Clara had rehearsed the speech so many times"
  3. "her hands trembled so badly that the pages rattled"
  4. "as she stepped onto the platform"
Answer C. Trembling hands that rattle the pages are a physical sign of nerves. The other quotations show preparation or simply describe her movement.
mock-2-rw2h-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Marine scientist Ibrahim Saleh hypothesises that a coral species survives warm water events better when it lives near seagrass meadows, because the seagrass lowers the acidity of the surrounding water.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Saleh's hypothesis?
  1. Warm water events have become more frequent in recent decades.
  2. Seagrass meadows provide shelter for many species of young fish.
  3. The coral species is found in warm waters around the world.
  4. During a recent warm event, colonies of the coral growing beside seagrass meadows, where water was measurably less acidic, lost far less coral than colonies of the same species growing away from seagrass.
Answer D. The hypothesis links survival near seagrass to lower acidity. Same-species colonies beside seagrass, in less acidic water, surviving better supports both parts of it.
mock-2-rw2h-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
A researcher surveyed adults about how many hours of sleep they usually get and how they rated their own concentration at work.


Sleep per nightRated concentration "good"Rated concentration "poor"
Less than 6 hours28%72%
6 to 7 hours55%45%
7 to 8 hours74%26%
More than 8 hours70%30%



The researcher concludes that, in this survey, more sleep was generally associated with better self-rated concentration, but that the benefit appeared to level off beyond a certain amount.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the researcher's conclusion?
  1. The share rating their concentration as good rose from 28% to 74% across the first three groups but was about the same for those sleeping more than 8 hours.
  2. Most adults who slept less than 6 hours rated their concentration as poor.
  3. The share rating their concentration as poor was 26% for those sleeping 7 to 8 hours.
  4. More adults slept 7 to 8 hours than any other amount.
Answer A. The conclusion has two parts: improvement with more sleep, then levelling off. Only the description of the rise across three groups followed by a similar figure (70%) for the longest sleepers covers both. The table does not give how many adults were in each group.
mock-2-rw2h-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
A city tracked the number of bike-share trips and the number of bike-share stations.


YearStationsTrips (thousands)
2019120600
2020150780
20212001,000
20222501,250



A transport official claims that each new station added roughly the same number of yearly trips. ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to support the official's claim?
  1. The number of stations doubled between 2019 and 2022.
  2. Across the years, trips stayed at about 5,000 per station, rising in step with the number of stations.
  3. The number of trips was highest in 2022.
  4. The city added 50 stations between 2021 and 2022.
