mock-2-rw2h-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
When glaciers melt, the land beneath them, freed of the ice's enormous weight, slowly rises, a process called isostatic rebound. In parts of northern Scandinavia, where thick ice sheets melted thousands of years ago, the land is still rising by up to a centimetre a year. Harbour structures built in some of these towns centuries ago now ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. have been crushed by the weight of new glaciers.
  2. lie deeper underwater than they did when they were built.
  3. stand further above the water than they did when they were built.
  4. show that the ice sheets never covered the region.
Answer C. If the land is still rising, structures built centuries ago have been lifted with it, so they now sit higher relative to the sea.
mock-2-rw2h-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Some plants release chemicals into the soil that slow the growth of nearby plants, a strategy called allelopathy. A researcher found that a certain shrub releases such chemicals only when its roots are crowded by roots of other species, not when it grows alone or among shrubs of its own kind. This suggests that the shrub ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. releases the chemicals at all times, whatever plants are nearby.
  2. cannot grow in soil without the chemicals.
  3. uses the chemicals to help shrubs of its own kind grow faster.
  4. releases the chemicals mainly in response to competition from other species.
Answer D. The shrub releases the chemicals only when crowded by other species, not alone or among its own kind, which points to a response to competition from other species.
mock-2-rw2h-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The expedition faced two main ______ the extreme cold and the difficulty of carrying enough food across the ice.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. challenges, which
  2. challenges;
  3. challenges
  4. challenges:
Answer D. A complete sentence introduces the two challenges, so a colon is used. A semicolon would need a complete sentence after it.
mock-2-rw2h-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The architect insisted that the museum's walls be made of local ______ she argued that the building should look as though it had grown out of the surrounding hills.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. stone;
  2. stone,
  3. stone
  4. stone, and,
Answer A. Both parts are complete sentences, so a semicolon is needed. A comma alone creates a comma splice.
mock-2-rw2h-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
In 1928, Alexander Fleming noticed that mould growing on one of his culture plates had killed the surrounding ______ a chance observation that led to the development of penicillin.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. bacteria;
  2. bacteria,
  3. bacteria.
  4. bacteria
Answer B. "A chance observation that led to..." is not a complete sentence; it describes what Fleming noticed. So it attaches with a comma, not a semicolon or full stop.
mock-2-rw2h-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The two scientists who first described the ______ worked in separate laboratories on different continents and did not learn of each other's research until both papers had been published.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. phenomenon;
  2. phenomenon,
  3. phenomenon
  4. phenomenon:
Answer C. "Who first described the phenomenon" identifies which scientists, and the subject "scientists" is followed directly by its verb "worked". No punctuation belongs between them.
mock-2-rw2h-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
The results of the survey, which was carried out in twelve countries over three years, ______ that most people now get their news online.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. is showing
  2. shows
  3. show
  4. has shown
Answer C. The subject is "results", which is plural; "of the survey" and the "which" clause only describe it. So the plural verb "show" is needed.
mock-2-rw2h-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Having studied the ruins for over a decade, ______
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. it was clear to the archaeologist that a drought had caused the city's abandonment.
  2. the city's abandonment after a drought seemed certain to the archaeologist.
  3. a drought was the archaeologist's explanation for the city's abandonment.
  4. the archaeologist was confident that the city had been abandoned after a drought.
Answer D. "Having studied the ruins for over a decade" must describe whoever studied them, so "the archaeologist" must come right after the comma.
mock-2-rw2h-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Neither the committee members nor the director ______ willing to approve the budget without further review.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. was
  2. were
  3. are
  4. have been
Answer A. With "neither... nor", the verb agrees with the nearer subject, "the director", which is singular, so "was" is needed.
mock-2-rw2h-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
The first version of the software was slow and often crashed. The developers rewrote much of the code. ______, the second version ran twice as fast and rarely failed.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Nevertheless
  2. Consequently
  3. For example
  4. Similarly
Answer B. The faster, more reliable second version resulted from rewriting the code, so "Consequently" fits.
mock-2-rw2h-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 3
Many early photographers saw their work as a scientific tool for recording the world accurately. Others, ______, used the camera to create dreamlike, artistic images that made no attempt at realism.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. in other words
  2. as a result
  3. by contrast
  4. for instance
Answer C. Dreamlike, unrealistic images contrast with accurate scientific records, so "by contrast" fits.
mock-2-rw2h-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Mary Anning (1799–1847) was a British fossil collector.
• She found the first complete plesiosaur skeleton in 1823.
• Because she was a woman, she could not join the Geological Society of London.
• Scientists often used her discoveries without crediting her.
The student wants to emphasise the obstacles Anning faced despite her achievements. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Geological Society of London studies fossils and rocks.
  2. Mary Anning was a British fossil collector who lived from 1799 to 1847.
  3. In 1823, Mary Anning found the first complete plesiosaur skeleton.
  4. Although Mary Anning found the first complete plesiosaur skeleton, she was barred from the Geological Society, and scientists often used her finds without credit.
Answer D. The goal pairs her achievement with the obstacles she faced. Only the sentence combining the plesiosaur find with her exclusion and lack of credit does both.
mock-2-rw2h-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Bamboo is a type of grass.
• Some species can grow up to 90 centimetres in a single day.
• It reaches full height in just a few months.
• Many builders use it because it is strong, light and quick to replace.
The student wants to explain why bamboo is a practical building material. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Because bamboo is strong and light and grows back quickly, many builders find it a practical material.
  2. Bamboo, a type of grass, can grow up to 90 centimetres in a single day.
  3. Bamboo reaches its full height in just a few months.
  4. There are many species of bamboo around the world.
Answer A. The goal is why builders find bamboo practical. Only the sentence giving its strength, lightness and quick regrowth as the reasons does that.

Math · Module 1 · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-2-m1-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $7x - 3 = 25$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $4$
  2. $5$
  3. $6$
  4. $7$
Answer A. Add $3$: $7x = 28$, so $x = 4$.
mock-2-m1-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 1
A machine fills $48$ bottles in $6$ minutes. At this rate, how many bottles does it fill in $15$ minutes?
  1. $90$
  2. $100$
  3. $110$
  4. $120$
Answer D. It fills $48 \div 6 = 8$ bottles a minute, so $15 \times 8 = 120$.
mock-2-m1-03 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = \dfrac{x}{2} + 9$. What is the value of $f(10)$?

Type-in answer: 14

Answer 14. $f(10) = \frac{10}{2} + 9 = 5 + 9 = 14$.
mock-2-m1-04 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 1
A square has a perimeter of $36$ centimetres. What is its area, in square centimetres?
  1. $36$
  2. $72$
  3. $81$
  4. $144$
Answer C. Each side is $36 \div 4 = 9$ cm, so the area is $9^2 = 81$.
mock-2-m1-05 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 1
Which expression is equivalent to $5x(2x - 3)$?
  1. $10x^2 - 15x$
  2. $10x^2 - 3$
  3. $7x^2 - 15x$
  4. $10x - 15x^2$
Answer A. Multiply each term by $5x$: $5x \cdot 2x - 5x \cdot 3 = 10x^2 - 15x$.
mock-2-m1-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
In a survey of $80$ people, $26$ said they prefer tea. What percentage of the people surveyed prefer tea?
  1. $26\%$
  2. $30\%$
  3. $32.5\%$
  4. $54\%$
Answer C. $\frac{26}{80} = 0.325 = 32.5\%$.
mock-2-m1-07 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 2
Tickets for a show cost $\$13$ for adults and $\$7$ for children. A group spends $\$89$ on $2$ adult tickets and $c$ child tickets. What is the value of $c$?
  1. $7$
  2. $8$
  3. $9$
  4. $10$
Answer C. $2(13) + 7c = 89$, so $26 + 7c = 89$, $7c = 63$ and $c = 9$.
mock-2-m1-08 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 2
Two vertical angles measure $(3x + 10)^\circ$ and $(5x - 30)^\circ$. What is the value of $x$?
  1. $20$
  2. $25$
  3. $30$
  4. $35$
Answer A. Vertical angles are equal: $3x + 10 = 5x - 30$, so $2x = 40$ and $x = 20$.
mock-2-m1-09 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$x + 3y = 14$
$2x - y = 7$


What is the value of $y$ in the solution to the system of equations above?

Type-in answer: 3

Answer 3. From the second equation, $y = 2x - 7$. Substitute: $x + 3(2x - 7) = 14$, so $7x = 35$, $x = 5$ and $y = 3$.
mock-2-m1-10 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $g$ is defined by $g(x) = x^2 - 3x$. What is the value of $g(-2)$?
  1. $-10$
  2. $-2$
  3. $2$
  4. $10$
Answer D. $g(-2) = (-2)^2 - 3(-2) = 4 + 6 = 10$.
mock-2-m1-11 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 2
What is the range of the data set $3, 8, 12, 15, 21$?
  1. $12$
  2. $15$
  3. $18$
  4. $21$
Answer C. Range $=$ greatest $-$ least $= 21 - 3 = 18$.
mock-2-m1-12 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
Which point $(x, y)$ satisfies the inequality $y > 2x + 1$?
  1. $(0, 0)$
  2. $(2, 6)$
  3. $(1, 2)$
  4. $(3, 6)$
Answer B. Test each: $(2, 6)$ gives $6 > 5$, true. $(0, 0)$ gives $0 > 1$, $(1, 2)$ gives $2 > 3$ and $(3, 6)$ gives $6 > 7$, all false.
mock-2-m1-13 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
If $(x - 3)^2 = 49$ and $x > 0$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 10

Answer 10. $x - 3 = 7$ or $x - 3 = -7$, so $x = 10$ or $x = -4$. Since $x > 0$, $x = 10$.
mock-2-m1-14 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
In right triangle $ABC$, angle $C$ is the right angle, $BC = 7$ and $AC = 24$. What is $\cos A$?
  1. $\dfrac{7}{25}$
  2. $\dfrac{24}{25}$
  3. $\dfrac{25}{24}$
  4. $\dfrac{24}{7}$
Answer B. The hypotenuse is $AB = \sqrt{7^2 + 24^2} = 25$. The side adjacent to $A$ is $AC = 24$, so $\cos A = \frac{24}{25}$.
mock-2-m1-15 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 2
The line of best fit $y = -2.4x + 30$ models the value $y$, in thousands of dollars, of a car that is $x$ years old. What is the predicted value of a $10$-year-old car?
  1. $\$6{,}000$
  2. $\$24{,}000$
  3. $\$27{,}600$
  4. $\$30{,}000$
Answer A. $y = -2.4(10) + 30 = 6$, which is $6$ thousand dollars.
mock-2-m1-16 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
Which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{x^2 + 2x - 15}{x - 3}$ for $x \ne 3$?
  1. $x - 5$
  2. $x + 5$
  3. $x + 3$
  4. $x - 3$
Answer B. $x^2 + 2x - 15 = (x + 5)(x - 3)$, and dividing by $x - 3$ leaves $x + 5$.
mock-2-m1-17 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 3
An item costing $\$60$ has its price increased by $15\%$. The new price is then decreased by $15\%$. What is the final price, in dollars?

Type-in answer: 58.65

Answer 58.65. $60 \times 1.15 = 69$, then $69 \times 0.85 = 58.65$.
mock-2-m1-18 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle in the $xy$-plane has equation $x^2 + y^2 + 8x - 6y = 0$. What are the coordinates of its centre?
  1. $(-4, 3)$
  2. $(4, -3)$
  3. $(-8, 6)$
  4. $(8, -6)$
Answer A. Complete the square: $(x + 4)^2 - 16 + (y - 3)^2 - 9 = 0$, so $(x + 4)^2 + (y - 3)^2 = 25$. The centre is $(-4, 3)$.
mock-2-m1-19 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 3
$ax + 3y = 6$
$4x + 6y = 5$


For what value of $a$ does the system of equations above have no solution?
  1. $1$
  2. $2$
  3. $3$
  4. $6$
Answer B. No solution means parallel lines: the coefficients must be in proportion, $\frac{a}{4} = \frac{3}{6}$, so $a = 2$. The constants ($6$ versus $\frac{5}{2}$ after scaling) do not match, so the lines are distinct.
mock-2-m1-20 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The function $f(x) = a(x - 2)(x + 4)$, where $a$ is a constant, has a graph that passes through $(0, -16)$. What is the value of $a$?
  1. $-2$
  2. $\dfrac{1}{2}$
  3. $2$
  4. $4$
Answer C. $f(0) = a(-2)(4) = -8a = -16$, so $a = 2$.
mock-2-m1-21 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A sphere has a volume of $288\pi$ cubic units. What is its radius?

Type-in answer: 6

Answer 6. $\frac{4}{3}\pi r^3 = 288\pi$, so $r^3 = 216$ and $r = 6$.
mock-2-m1-22 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
Two fair coins are tossed. What is the probability of getting at least one head?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{4}$
  2. $\dfrac{1}{2}$
  3. $\dfrac{2}{3}$
  4. $\dfrac{3}{4}$
Answer D. The outcomes are HH, HT, TH and TT. Only TT has no head, so the probability is $1 - \frac{1}{4} = \frac{3}{4}$.

mock-2-m2h-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 2
If $\dfrac{2x + 1}{3} - \dfrac{x - 1}{2} = 2$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $5$
  2. $7$
  3. $9$
  4. $11$
Answer B. Multiply by $6$: $2(2x + 1) - 3(x - 1) = 12$, so $4x + 2 - 3x + 3 = 12$ and $x = 7$.
mock-2-m2h-02 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 3(2)^x$. What is the value of $\dfrac{f(x + 1)}{f(x)}$?

Type-in answer: 2

Answer 2. $\frac{3(2)^{x+1}}{3(2)^x} = 2^{(x + 1) - x} = 2$.
mock-2-m2h-03 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 3
$3x + 2y = 7$
$5x - 2y = 9$


If $(x, y)$ is the solution to the system of equations above, what is the value of $x - y$?

Type-in answer: 1.5 or 3/2

Answer 1.5. Add the equations: $8x = 16$, so $x = 2$. Then $6 + 2y = 7$ gives $y = \frac{1}{2}$, and $x - y = \frac{3}{2}$.
mock-2-m2h-04 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 3
A price is increased by $25\%$. By what percentage must the new price be decreased to return to the original price?
  1. $20\%$
  2. $25\%$
  3. $30\%$
  4. $80\%$
Answer A. New price $= 1.25p$. To get back to $p$, multiply by $\frac{1}{1.25} = 0.8$: a $20\%$ decrease.
mock-2-m2h-05 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
If $x + \dfrac{1}{x} = 5$, what is the value of $x^2 + \dfrac{1}{x^2}$?
  1. $21$
  2. $23$
  3. $25$
  4. $27$
Answer B. Square both sides: $x^2 + 2 + \frac{1}{x^2} = 25$, so $x^2 + \frac{1}{x^2} = 23$.
mock-2-m2h-06 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 3

  1. $\dfrac{3}{5}$
  2. $\dfrac{4}{5}$
  3. $\dfrac{3}{4}$
  4. $\dfrac{5}{3}$
Answer A. Use a right triangle with opposite $3$ and adjacent $4$; the hypotenuse is $5$. So $\sin\theta = \frac{3}{5}$.
mock-2-m2h-07 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
What is the solution to the equation $\sqrt{2x + 7} = x + 2$?

Type-in answer: 1

Answer 1. Square: $2x + 7 = x^2 + 4x + 4$, so $x^2 + 2x - 3 = 0$ and $x = 1$ or $x = -3$. Check: $x = -3$ gives $\sqrt{1} = 1$ but $x + 2 = -1$, so it fails. The solution is $x = 1$.
mock-2-m2h-08 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 3
Four numbers have a mean of $15$. A fifth number, $25$, is added. What is the mean of the five numbers?
  1. $15$
  2. $16$
  3. $17$
  4. $20$
Answer C. The four numbers total $60$. With $25$ added the total is $85$, and $85 \div 5 = 17$.
mock-2-m2h-09 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 3
Line $k$ passes through $(-2, 5)$ and is parallel to the line $y = -3x + 1$. What is the $y$-intercept of line $k$?
  1. $-11$
  2. $-1$
  3. $1$
  4. $11$
Answer B. Line $k$ has slope $-3$: $y - 5 = -3(x + 2)$, so $y = -3x - 1$. The $y$-intercept is $-1$.
mock-2-m2h-10 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The height, in feet, of a ball $t$ seconds after it is thrown is $h(t) = -16t^2 + 64t$. After how many seconds does the ball hit the ground?
  1. $2$
  2. $4$
  3. $8$
  4. $16$
Answer B. Set $h(t) = 0$: $-16t(t - 4) = 0$, so $t = 0$ (the throw) or $t = 4$.
mock-2-m2h-11 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 3
One gallon of paint covers $350$ square feet. A wall is $14$ feet high and $50$ feet long and needs two coats of paint. How many gallons of paint are needed?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. Area $= 14 \times 50 = 700$ square feet; two coats need $1{,}400$ square feet, and $1{,}400 \div 350 = 4$ gallons.
mock-2-m2h-12 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A sector of a circle of radius $6$ has an area of $12\pi$. What is the measure, in degrees, of the sector's central angle?
  1. $60$
  2. $90$
  3. $120$
  4. $150$
Answer C. The whole circle has area $36\pi$, so the sector is $\frac{12\pi}{36\pi} = \frac{1}{3}$ of it: $\frac{1}{3} \times 360 = 120$.
mock-2-m2h-13 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
A box contains $5$ red and $3$ blue counters. Two counters are drawn at random without replacement. What is the probability that both are red?
  1. $\dfrac{5}{14}$
  2. $\dfrac{25}{64}$
  3. $\dfrac{5}{8}$
  4. $\dfrac{9}{14}$
Answer A. $P = \frac{5}{8} \times \frac{4}{7} = \frac{20}{56} = \frac{5}{14}$.
mock-2-m2h-14 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
For $x > 0$, which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{x^{3/2} \cdot x^{1/2}}{x}$?
  1. $x$
  2. $x^2$
  3. $x^{3/4}$
  4. $x^{1/2}$
Answer A. Add the exponents on top: $x^{3/2 + 1/2} = x^2$. Then $\frac{x^2}{x} = x$.
mock-2-m2h-15 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 3
A phone plan costs $\$25$ a month plus $\$0.10$ for each text. Maya wants her monthly bill to be at most $\$40$. What is the greatest number of texts she can send in a month?
  1. $140$
  2. $149$
  3. $150$
  4. $160$
Answer C. $25 + 0.10t \le 40$ gives $0.10t \le 15$, so $t \le 150$.
mock-2-m2h-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Studies and experiments · difficulty 3
A researcher surveyed $200$ people at a city gym and found that $85\%$ exercise at least four times a week. Which statement about the survey is most appropriate?
  1. About 85% of adults in the city exercise at least four times a week.
  2. The gym causes people to exercise more often.
  3. Most adults in the country exercise at least four times a week.
  4. The result cannot be generalised to all adults in the city, because the people surveyed were not a random sample of city adults.
Answer D. Gym users are likely to exercise more than other people, so the sample is not representative of all adults, and a survey cannot show cause and effect.
mock-2-m2h-17 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A cube has a total surface area of $150$ square centimetres. What is its volume, in cubic centimetres?

Type-in answer: 125

Answer 125. Six equal faces: each is $150 \div 6 = 25$, so the edge is $5$ and the volume is $5^3 = 125$.
mock-2-m2h-18 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 3
The function $P(x) = 200(1.05)^x$ models the population, in thousands, of a town $x$ years after 2020. What does the number $1.05$ mean in this context?
  1. The population increases by 1,050 people each year.
  2. The population in 2020 was 105,000.
  3. The population grows by 5% each year.
  4. The population will reach 1.05 million.
Answer C. Multiplying by $1.05$ each year means each year's population is $105\%$ of the year before: $5\%$ growth.
mock-2-m2h-19 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 3
Triangle $ABC$ is similar to triangle $DEF$, with $A$, $B$, $C$ corresponding to $D$, $E$, $F$. If $AB = 6$, $DE = 9$ and $BC = 8$, what is $EF$?
  1. $10$
  2. $12$
  3. $13.5$
  4. $16$
Answer B. The scale factor is $\frac{9}{6} = 1.5$, so $EF = 8 \times 1.5 = 12$.
mock-2-m2h-20 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 3
A poll estimates that $40\%$ of voters support a proposal, with a margin of error of $3\%$. Which of the following is a plausible value for the true percentage?
  1. $42\%$
  2. $35\%$
  3. $44\%$
  4. $46\%$
Answer A. The plausible range is $40\% \pm 3\%$: $37\%$ to $43\%$. Only $42\%$ is in that range.
mock-2-m2h-21 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
The graph of $y = x^2 - 6x + k$ touches the $x$-axis at exactly one point. What is the value of $k$?
  1. $3$
  2. $6$
  3. $9$
  4. $36$
Answer C. Exactly one $x$-intercept means the discriminant is zero: $36 - 4k = 0$, so $k = 9$.
mock-2-m2h-22 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 2
A line passes through $(1, 1)$ and $(3, 7)$. What is the value of $y$ on this line when $x = 5$?
  1. $7$
  2. $9$
  3. $11$
  4. $13$
Answer D. Slope $= \frac{7 - 1}{3 - 1} = 3$, so $y = 3x - 2$. When $x = 5$, $y = 13$.

SAT Practice Test 3

Reading and Writing · Module 1 · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-3-rw1-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
After weeks of rehearsal, the choir's performance was ______: every singer came in on time, and the harmonies were perfectly balanced.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. flawless
  2. hesitant
  3. disorganised
  4. brief
Answer A. Every singer on time and perfectly balanced harmonies describe a performance with no mistakes: "flawless".
mock-3-rw1-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The new park quickly became ______ with local families, who filled its playgrounds and picnic areas every weekend.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. unknown
  2. popular
  3. unsafe
  4. expensive
Answer B. Families filling it every weekend shows it was well liked: "popular".
mock-3-rw1-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The author's prose is ______; she rarely uses a long word where a short one will do, and her sentences seldom run past a single line.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. obscure
  2. ornate
  3. economical
  4. repetitive
Answer C. Short words and short sentences describe writing that uses no more than it needs: "economical". "Ornate" means highly decorated, the opposite.
mock-3-rw1-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Although the two scientists disagreed sharply in public, their private letters reveal a surprisingly ______ relationship, full of shared jokes and requests for advice.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. fleeting
  2. hostile
  3. formal
  4. cordial
Answer D. "Although" contrasts their public disagreement with their private letters, which are full of jokes and requests for advice: a friendly, "cordial" relationship.
mock-3-rw1-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 1
Honeyguides are African birds that lead people to wild bees' nests. The birds call and flutter from tree to tree until the people find the nest and open it. The people take the honey, and the birds eat the leftover wax.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To compare honeyguides with other African birds
  2. To argue that bees should be protected
  3. To explain how bees build their nests
  4. To describe a partnership between a bird and people
Answer D. The text describes birds guiding people to nests, the people taking honey and the birds eating the wax: a partnership that benefits both.
mock-3-rw1-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
Early maps of the world often included imaginary islands. Some appeared because sailors mistook fog banks or icebergs for land; others were copied from older maps without being checked. A few such islands stayed on official charts into the twentieth century.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It describes a feature of early maps, explains how it arose and notes how long it lasted.
  2. It argues that early mapmakers were careless and should not be trusted.
  3. It compares early maps with modern satellite images.
  4. It tells the story of a single explorer who discovered an island.
Answer A. The text introduces imaginary islands, gives two reasons they appeared, and ends by noting some lasted into the twentieth century.
mock-3-rw1-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
The following text is adapted from a 1910 novel. Edith is waiting for a letter.


Every morning Edith met the postman at the gate, and every morning he shook his head before she could ask. By the second week she had stopped asking; she simply stood there until he passed, and then went back inside to sit by the window, where she could see the road.
Which choice best describes the function of the second sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It explains why the postman dislikes Edith.
  2. It shows how Edith's hope continues even as she stops expecting an answer from the postman.
  3. It reveals what the letter Edith is waiting for will say.
  4. It suggests that Edith has decided to leave home.
Answer B. She stops asking yet still waits at the gate and watches the road, which shows her continued, quieter hope. Nothing reveals the letter's contents or any plan to leave.
mock-3-rw1-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Many people believe that multitasking, such as checking messages while studying, lets them get more done.


Text 2
In experiments, people who switched between tasks made more mistakes and took longer to finish than people who completed one task before starting another. Researchers say the brain loses time every time it switches focus.
Based on the texts, how would the researchers in Text 2 most likely respond to the belief in Text 1?
  1. By claiming that checking messages improves concentration
  2. By agreeing that multitasking helps people finish more work
  3. By arguing that multitasking usually makes people slower and less accurate
  4. By suggesting that the brain never switches focus
Answer C. Their experiments found switching between tasks caused more mistakes and slower finishing, which contradicts the belief.
mock-3-rw1-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
The Sahara has not always been a desert. Rock paintings found deep in it show giraffes, hippos and herds of cattle, and scientists have found the remains of ancient lakes. About 6,000 years ago, changes in Earth's orbit shifted rain patterns, and the region slowly dried out.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Rock paintings are the oldest form of art.
  2. Hippos still live in parts of the Sahara.
  3. Evidence shows the Sahara was once much wetter before it became a desert.
  4. Earth's orbit has never changed.
Answer C. The paintings of water-loving animals and ancient lakes show the Sahara was once wetter, and the text explains how it dried out.
mock-3-rw1-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from a 1904 short story.


Old Mr. Okafor was known in the village for two things: the tallest mango tree for miles around, and his refusal to sell a single mango. Every season he gave the fruit away, a basket to each house, and when people offered to pay he only laughed and asked them to bring the seeds back.
According to the text, what does Mr. Okafor do with his mangoes?
  1. He trades them for seeds from other farmers.
  2. He sells them at the market.
  3. He keeps them for his own family.
  4. He gives them away to the people of the village.
Answer D. He gives "a basket to each house" and laughs when people offer to pay. He only asks for the seeds back; he is not trading for other farmers' seeds.
mock-3-rw1-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
A researcher claims that working in a room with plants improves people's mood.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support the researcher's claim?
  1. Office workers randomly assigned to rooms with plants reported better moods over a month than workers assigned to identical rooms without plants.
  2. Many offices now include plants in their design.
  3. Plants need regular watering to stay healthy.
  4. Some people are allergic to certain plants.
Answer A. Random assignment to otherwise identical rooms, with better moods in the rooms with plants, directly supports the claim.
mock-3-rw1-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Anthropologist Laila Haddad argues that an ancient settlement was abandoned gradually over several generations, rather than suddenly after a disaster.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Haddad's argument?
  1. The settlement was built near a river.
  2. Houses in the settlement were emptied of tools and pottery one by one over about eighty years, while the others stayed in use.
  3. Archaeologists found a layer of ash covering the whole settlement.
  4. Other settlements in the region were larger.
Answer B. Houses being emptied one by one over eighty years shows a slow departure. A layer of ash over everything would instead suggest a sudden disaster.

mock-3-rw1-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
Researchers compared the average yearly cost of running two kinds of car.


CostPetrol carElectric car
Fuel or charging$1,900$650
Maintenance$900$500
Insurance$1,100$1,300



The researchers conclude that although insurance costs more for the electric car, its total yearly running cost is still lower.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the researchers' conclusion?
  1. The electric car's total of $2,450 is lower than the petrol car's $3,900, despite its higher insurance.
  2. Insurance cost $1,300 for the electric car.
  3. Fuel was the largest cost for the petrol car.
  4. Maintenance cost less for the electric car than for the petrol car.

mock-3-rw1-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
In some deserts, beetles stand on sand dunes at dawn with their backs tilted into the wind. Fog drifting in from the sea condenses into droplets on their bumpy shells, and the droplets run down toward their mouths. This suggests that the beetles' behaviour helps them ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. stay cool during the hottest part of the day.
  2. collect drinking water in a dry environment.
  3. hide from predators on the dunes.
  4. find food buried in the sand.
Answer B. Fog condenses on their shells and runs to their mouths, so the behaviour lets them drink in the desert.
mock-3-rw1-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
A study compared two groups of patients recovering from the same operation. One group had a window view of trees; the other faced a brick wall. The tree-view group needed fewer painkillers and left hospital earlier on average. The patients were assigned to rooms based only on which beds were free. If the study's findings hold, hospitals designing new wards might ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. build all new wards facing brick walls.
  2. replace painkillers with pictures of trees.
  3. consider giving patients views of nature to support recovery.
  4. conclude that room assignment affects which operation patients need.
Answer C. Patients with a tree view recovered better, and rooms were assigned by chance, which strengthens the link. So the practical step is to give patients views of nature. Replacing painkillers goes far beyond the evidence.

mock-3-rw1-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 1

  4. As a result

mock-3-rw1-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2

  1. Therefore
  2. However
  3. Similarly
  4. In other words
Answer B. Evidence of fast, warm-blooded-style growth contrasts with the cold-blooded view, so "However" fits.
mock-3-rw1-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Silk Road was a network of trade routes.
• It linked China with the Mediterranean.
• Merchants traded silk, spices and paper.
• Ideas and religions also spread along it.
The student wants to emphasise that the Silk Road spread more than goods. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Merchants traded silk on the Silk Road.
  2. The Silk Road linked China with the Mediterranean.
  3. Along with silk, spices and paper, ideas and religions also spread along the Silk Road.
  4. The Silk Road was a network of trade routes.
Answer C. Only the sentence mentioning ideas and religions, beyond goods, meets the goal.
mock-3-rw1-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Tardigrades are tiny animals about 0.5 mm long.
• They can survive being frozen, dried out and exposed to radiation.
• In 2007, tardigrades survived ten days in open space.
• They do this by entering a state where their metabolism almost stops.
The student wants to explain how tardigrades survive extreme conditions. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Tardigrades can survive being frozen and dried out.
  2. In 2007, tardigrades survived ten days in open space.
  3. Tardigrades are tiny animals about 0.5 mm long.
  4. Tardigrades survive extreme conditions by entering a state in which their metabolism almost stops.
Answer D. The goal is how they survive; only the sentence describing the near-stopped metabolism explains the mechanism.

mock-3-rw2e-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Some people think that eating carrots greatly improves eyesight.


Text 2
Carrots contain vitamin A, which the eyes need to work properly, so people who lack vitamin A can see better after eating them. But for people who already get enough vitamin A, eating more carrots does not make their eyesight sharper.
Based on the texts, how would the author of Text 2 most likely respond to the belief in Text 1?
  1. By claiming that carrots harm eyesight
  2. By agreeing that carrots improve everyone's eyesight
  3. By explaining that carrots help eyesight mainly for people lacking vitamin A
  4. By arguing that vitamin A has nothing to do with eyes
Answer C. Text 2 says carrots help only people short of vitamin A, so it would limit the belief rather than accept or reject it entirely.

  3. its'
  4. its
Answer D. The paws belong to the cat, so the possessive "its" is used. "It's" means "it is".
mock-3-rw2e-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 1
The library is closed today. ______, the reading room will open tomorrow at nine.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. However
  2. For example
  3. Similarly
  4. As a result
Answer A. Opening tomorrow contrasts with being closed today, so "However" fits.
mock-3-rw2e-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
Bees collect nectar from flowers. ______, they carry pollen from plant to plant, which helps plants make seeds.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Instead
  2. In contrast
  3. At the same time
  4. Nevertheless
Answer C. Carrying pollen happens while bees collect nectar, so "At the same time" fits.
mock-3-rw2e-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 1
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Nile is a river in Africa.
• It flows north into the Mediterranean Sea.
• It is about 6,650 km long.
The student wants to state where the Nile ends. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Nile is one of the world's rivers.
  2. The Nile is a river in Africa.
  3. The Nile is about 6,650 km long.
  4. The Nile flows north into the Mediterranean Sea.
Answer D. Only the sentence naming the Mediterranean Sea states where the river ends.
mock-3-rw2e-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Polar bears have black skin under their fur.
• Their fur is not actually white; each hair is clear and hollow.
• The hairs scatter light, so the fur looks white.
The student wants to explain why polar bears look white. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Polar bears look white because their clear, hollow hairs scatter light.
  2. Polar bears have black skin under their fur.
  3. Polar bear fur is not actually white.
  4. Polar bears live in the Arctic.
Answer A. Only the sentence linking the clear, hollow hairs scattering light to the white look explains it.

Reading and Writing · Module 2 (harder) · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-3-rw2h-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The biography is unusually ______: rather than praising its subject, the author records the inventor's failures and cruelties as carefully as his achievements.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. candid
  2. flattering
  3. speculative
  4. abridged
Answer A. Recording failures and cruelties as carefully as achievements is frank and honest: "candid". "Flattering" is the opposite.
mock-3-rw2h-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Far from being ______, the town's ban on cars in the old centre was the result of fifteen years of public meetings, traffic studies and trial closures.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. deliberate
  2. impulsive
  3. permanent
  4. controversial
Answer B. "Far from" signals the opposite of the evidence that follows. Fifteen years of meetings and studies show careful planning, so the ban was far from "impulsive".
mock-3-rw2h-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Early reviewers found the composer's harmonies ______, but audiences a generation later heard in them a beauty that the critics had been too unsettled to notice.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. conventional
  2. soothing
  3. jarring
  4. imitative
Answer C. The critics were "unsettled", so they found the harmonies harsh: "jarring". "Soothing" and "conventional" would not unsettle anyone.
mock-3-rw2h-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The diplomat's reply was a model of ______: in three short sentences, she declined the proposal, thanked its authors and left the door open to future talks.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. indecision
  2. bluntness
  3. verbosity
  4. tact
Answer D. Declining, thanking and keeping talks possible, all briefly and politely, shows skill in handling a delicate situation: "tact". "Verbosity" contradicts "three short sentences".
mock-3-rw2h-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1917 poem.


The river does not hurry, yet it reaches
The sea it never saw, and never sought;
It finds its way through stones and sand and beaches
By going on, and not by any thought.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It describes the plants and animals found along a river.
  2. It contrasts a calm river with a stormy sea.
  3. It tells the story of a journey made by the speaker along a river.
  4. It describes a river's steady progress and suggests that persistence, not planning, carries it to its goal.
Answer D. The river reaches the sea "by going on, and not by any thought": the poem shows steady movement succeeding without a plan.
mock-3-rw2h-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Scholars have long read the explorer's journals as a record of a lonely journey through empty land. Historian Ruth Banda argues that this reading overlooks the dozens of local guides, interpreters and porters whom the explorer mentions only in passing, often without names. Without them, she contends, the expedition would not have survived its first month.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To present an argument that the explorer's journey depended on people his journals largely ignored
  2. To describe the landscape the explorer crossed
  3. To show that the explorer's journals were written by someone else
  4. To argue that the expedition failed in its first month
Answer A. The text introduces Banda's challenge to the "lonely journey" reading: the guides and porters, barely mentioned, were essential. It does not say the expedition failed or the journals were faked.
mock-3-rw2h-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Many birds sing complex songs, and for decades researchers assumed that only males sang. Then a survey of over a thousand songbird species found that females sing in most of them. The early assumption, it turned out, reflected where researchers had looked: mostly in Europe and North America, where female song is relatively rare.
Which choice best describes the function of the third sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It describes the songs that female birds sing.
  2. It explains why researchers had held a mistaken assumption.
  3. It questions the results of the survey mentioned in the second sentence.
  4. It argues that European birds sing more than others.
Answer B. The first two sentences give the old assumption and the evidence against it; the third explains where the mistake came from.
mock-3-rw2h-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
Proponents of "learning styles" claim that students learn best when lessons match their preferred style, such as visual or auditory. Teachers, they argue, should identify each student's style and teach accordingly.


Text 2
When researchers taught students in either their preferred style or a different one, test results did not differ. What helped most was matching the method to the content: diagrams for maps, for instance, and spoken examples for pronunciation.
Based on the texts, how would the researchers in Text 2 most likely respond to the proponents in Text 1?
  1. By claiming that visual lessons are always best
  2. By agreeing that teachers should identify each student's learning style
  3. By arguing that the type of content matters more than a student's preferred style
  4. By suggesting that students should choose what they study
Answer C. The study found matching students' preferred styles made no difference, while matching method to content helped, so the researchers would put content ahead of learning styles.
mock-3-rw2h-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is adapted from Elizabeth Gaskell's 1855 novel North and South.


Margaret had been accustomed to the quiet, sunny lanes of the south. In the northern town, the sky was low and grey with smoke, and the streets were crowded with workers hurrying to the mills. She felt the change as a heaviness she could not at first explain.
Which choice best describes Margaret's response to the northern town?
  1. She finds the town quieter than she expected.
  2. She is delighted by the busy streets and mills.
  3. She is eager to work in one of the mills.
  4. She feels weighed down by how different it is from the south she knew.
Answer D. Used to quiet, sunny lanes, she feels "a heaviness" in the smoky, crowded town, a response to the contrast.
mock-3-rw2h-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 3
Economists studying a city's new minimum wage expected restaurants to cut jobs. Instead, employment in restaurants stayed steady. Many owners reported that higher pay reduced how often workers quit, which cut the costs of hiring and training new staff. The economists suggest that for some businesses, lower staff turnover can partly offset the cost of higher wages.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. A higher minimum wage did not reduce restaurant jobs, partly because it lowered staff turnover costs.
  2. Restaurants in the city closed because of higher wages.
  3. Economists always predict job losses correctly.
  4. Training new staff costs nothing for most restaurants.
Answer A. Jobs held steady against expectations, and owners linked higher pay to fewer resignations and lower hiring costs, which the economists say partly offsets wages.
mock-3-rw2h-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
The following text is from a 1909 novel. The narrator describes her brother.


Thomas could never sit still. At dinner he tapped the table; in church he counted the windows; and on the long train journey to the coast he walked the length of the carriage so often that the guard began to greet him by name.
Which quotation from the text most effectively illustrates the narrator's claim about Thomas?
  1. "on the long train journey to the coast"
  2. "he walked the length of the carriage so often that the guard began to greet him by name"
  3. "The narrator describes her brother"
  4. "in church"
Answer B. The claim is that he could never sit still; walking the carriage so often that the guard knew him illustrates it. The other quotations only give places or the introduction.
mock-3-rw2h-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Neuroscientist Hana Mori hypothesises that a nap after learning helps people remember new information, because during sleep the brain replays recently learned material.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Mori's hypothesis?
  1. Some people find it hard to nap during the day.
  2. Many people feel more refreshed after a short nap.
  3. Volunteers who napped after learning word pairs remembered more of them than volunteers who stayed awake, and brain scans showed patterns from the learning session recurring during their naps.
  4. Volunteers remembered more words in the morning than in the evening.
Answer C. The hypothesis has two parts: naps improve memory, and the brain replays the material. Better recall after napping plus learning-session patterns recurring during sleep supports both.
mock-3-rw2h-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
A city compared commuting before and after opening a new tram line.


ModeShare of commuters beforeShare after
Car52%41%
Bus22%18%
Tram0%17%
Walk or cycle26%24%



A planner claims that most new tram riders switched from cars rather than from other ways of travelling.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the planner's claim?
  1. The car share fell by 11 percentage points, while the bus and walk-or-cycle shares fell by only 4 and 2.
  2. The tram carried 17% of commuters after it opened.
  3. Cars were the most common mode both before and after.
  4. Walking and cycling made up about a quarter of commuting.
Answer A. The tram took 17 points. Of the losses, cars fell 11 points, buses 4 and walking or cycling 2, so most of the tram's share came from cars.
mock-3-rw2h-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
Researchers tracked a lake's water clarity, measured as how deep a white disc stays visible.

The researchers suggest that the fertiliser rule, which reduces nutrients washing into the lake, may have helped clear the water. ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to support the researchers' suggestion?
  1. Visibility was 3.6 m in 2022.
  2. As the share of farms following the rule rose from 10% to 85%, visibility increased from 2.1 m to 3.6 m.
  3. In 2018, 35% of farms followed the rule.
  4. Visibility was lowest in 2016.
