mock-3-rw2h-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Some mountain plants flower only once in their lives, often after decades of growth, and then die. One such plant stores sugars in its thick roots for many years before producing a single tall flower spike. Researchers found that plants growing in richer soil flowered several years earlier than plants in poorer soil. This suggests that the timing of flowering may depend on ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. how many other plants grow nearby.
  2. how tall the plant's flower spike will be.
  3. how quickly the plant can build up enough stored energy.
  4. the age of the oldest plant in the area.
Answer C. The plant stores sugars for years before flowering, and plants in richer soil (which could build reserves faster) flowered earlier. So timing likely depends on reaching enough stored energy.
mock-3-rw2h-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
In a trial, a supermarket moved fruit from the back of the store to the entrance. Fruit sales rose by a third, while sales of sweets, which were not moved, stayed the same. Shoppers' total spending did not change. The trial suggests that the new layout ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. had no effect on what shoppers bought.
  2. persuaded shoppers to stop buying sweets.
  3. increased the amount shoppers spent on every visit.
  4. shifted some spending toward fruit without making shoppers spend more overall.
Answer D. Fruit sales rose but total spending did not, so money moved to fruit from other items (not sweets, which stayed the same). Shoppers did not spend more overall.
mock-3-rw2h-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The archive contains three kinds of ______ handwritten letters, printed pamphlets and early photographs.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. material:
  2. material;
  3. material
  4. material, which
Answer A. A complete sentence introduces the list, so a colon is correct.
mock-3-rw2h-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The software update fixed the security ______ it also made the app noticeably slower on older phones.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. flaw, however,
  2. flaw; however,
  3. flaw however
  4. flaw; however
Answer B. "However" begins a new complete sentence, so a semicolon goes before it and a comma after it.
mock-3-rw2h-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
In 1953, Tenzing Norgay and Edmund Hillary reached the summit of ______ a climb that made them famous around the world.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. Everest.
  2. Everest;
  3. Everest,
  4. Everest
Answer C. "A climb that made them famous" is not a complete sentence; it describes the event and attaches with a comma.
mock-3-rw2h-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The engineers who designed the original ______ never imagined that it would one day carry four times as much traffic.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. bridge:
  2. bridge,
  3. bridge;
  4. bridge
Answer D. "Who designed the original bridge" identifies which engineers, and "engineers" is followed by its verb "imagined". No punctuation belongs between them.
mock-3-rw2h-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
The collection of fossils, which includes several complete skeletons, ______ attracted researchers from around the world.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. are
  2. have
  3. has
  4. were
Answer C. The subject is "collection", which is singular; "of fossils" and the "which" clause describe it. So "has" is needed.
mock-3-rw2h-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Exhausted after the long climb, ______
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. resting beside the lake came before the hikers started down.
  2. the lake was where the hikers rested before starting down.
  3. a rest beside the lake was taken before starting down.
  4. the hikers rested beside the lake before starting down.
Answer D. "Exhausted after the long climb" must describe the hikers, so "the hikers" must come right after the comma.
mock-3-rw2h-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
Neither the manager nor her assistants ______ aware of the problem until a customer pointed it out.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. were
  2. was
  3. is
  4. has been
Answer A. With "neither... nor", the verb agrees with the nearer subject, "assistants", which is plural, so "were" is needed.
mock-3-rw2h-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 2
The first vaccines were developed against smallpox in the late 1700s. ______, it took almost two centuries of vaccination campaigns before the disease was finally eliminated worldwide in 1980.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. As a result
  2. Even so
  3. For example
  4. Similarly
Answer B. Despite an early vaccine, elimination took two centuries: a contrast that "Even so" signals.
mock-3-rw2h-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 3
Some economists argue that cities should charge drivers to enter congested centres. Such charges, ______, have reduced traffic in several cities, though critics say they fall hardest on people with lower incomes.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. instead
  2. by contrast
  3. in practice
  4. nonetheless
Answer C. The sentence moves from the economists' argument to what charges have done in real cities, so "in practice" fits.
mock-3-rw2h-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Hedy Lamarr was a film star of the 1930s and 1940s.
• During the Second World War, she co-invented a radio system that switched frequencies to stop signals being jammed.
• The navy did not use it at the time.
• Its principle was later used in technologies such as Wi-Fi and Bluetooth.
The student wants to emphasise the long-term importance of Lamarr's invention. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The navy did not use Lamarr's invention at the time.
  2. Hedy Lamarr was a film star of the 1930s and 1940s.
  3. During the Second World War, Lamarr co-invented a radio system.
  4. Although the navy did not use it at first, the frequency-switching principle Lamarr co-invented was later used in Wi-Fi and Bluetooth.
Answer D. Long-term importance means its later use. Only the sentence linking the principle to Wi-Fi and Bluetooth shows that.
mock-3-rw2h-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• Coral reefs cover less than 1% of the ocean floor.
• They are home to about 25% of all marine species.
• Rising sea temperatures can cause coral bleaching.
• Bleached corals can recover if temperatures drop quickly.
The student wants to emphasise how important coral reefs are for ocean life relative to their size. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. Although coral reefs cover less than 1% of the ocean floor, they are home to about a quarter of all marine species.
  2. Rising sea temperatures can cause coral bleaching.
  3. Bleached corals can recover if temperatures drop quickly.
  4. Coral reefs are found in the ocean.
Answer A. Only the sentence comparing their small area with the share of species they support meets the goal.

Math · Module 1 · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-3-m1-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $4x + 9 = 33$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $6$
  2. $7$
  3. $8$
  4. $9$
Answer A. Subtract $9$: $4x = 24$, so $x = 6$.
mock-3-m1-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 1
A car travels $150$ miles in $3$ hours. At this rate, how many miles does it travel in $5$ hours?
  1. $200$
  2. $225$
  3. $250$
  4. $300$
Answer C. The car travels $150 \div 3 = 50$ miles an hour, so $5 \times 50 = 250$ miles.
mock-3-m1-03 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 3x - 4$. What is the value of $f(5)$?

Type-in answer: 11

Answer 11. $f(5) = 3(5) - 4 = 15 - 4 = 11$.
mock-3-m1-04 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 1
A rectangle is $8$ metres long and $5$ metres wide. What is its area, in square metres?
  1. $26$
  2. $40$
  3. $45$
  4. $80$
Answer B. Area $=$ length $\times$ width $= 8 \times 5 = 40$. The value $26$ is the perimeter.
mock-3-m1-05 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 1
Which expression is equivalent to $(x + 4)(x - 2)$?
  1. $x^2 + 2x - 8$
  2. $x^2 - 2x - 8$
  3. $x^2 + 6x - 8$
  4. $x^2 + 2x + 8$
Answer A. Expand: $x^2 - 2x + 4x - 8 = x^2 + 2x - 8$.
mock-3-m1-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
What is $15\%$ of $240$?
  1. $36$
  2. $40$
  3. $48$
  4. $60$
Answer A. $0.15 \times 240 = 36$.
mock-3-m1-07 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 2
A gym charges a $\$20$ joining fee and $\$12$ per month. A member has paid $\$116$ in total for $m$ months, including the joining fee. What is the value of $m$?
  1. $7$
  2. $8$
  3. $9$
  4. $10$
Answer B. $20 + 12m = 116$, so $12m = 96$ and $m = 8$.
mock-3-m1-08 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 2
Two angles of a triangle measure $40^\circ$ and $75^\circ$. What is the measure of the third angle?
  1. $65^\circ$
  2. $75^\circ$
  3. $105^\circ$
  4. $115^\circ$
Answer A. The angles of a triangle add to $180^\circ$: $180 - 40 - 75 = 65$.
mock-3-m1-09 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$x + y = 11$
$x - y = 3$


What is the value of $x$ in the solution to the system of equations above?

Type-in answer: 7

Answer 7. Add the equations: $2x = 14$, so $x = 7$ (and $y = 4$).
mock-3-m1-10 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $h$ is defined by $h(x) = 2x^2 - 5$. What is the value of $h(3)$?
  1. $-1$
  2. $7$
  3. $13$
  4. $31$
Answer C. $h(3) = 2(3^2) - 5 = 18 - 5 = 13$.
mock-3-m1-11 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 2
What is the mean of the data set $4, 7, 9, 12, 13$?
  1. $6$
  2. $7$
  3. $8$
  4. $9$
Answer D. The sum is $4 + 7 + 9 + 12 + 13 = 45$, and $45 \div 5 = 9$.
mock-3-m1-12 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
Which value of $x$ is a solution to the inequality $4x + 3 > 19$?
  1. $2$
  2. $3$
  3. $4$
  4. $5$
Answer D. Subtract $3$ and divide by $4$: $x > 4$. Only $5$ is greater than $4$; $x = 4$ gives $19 > 19$, which is false.
mock-3-m1-13 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
If $x^2 = 3x + 10$ and $x > 0$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. Rearrange: $x^2 - 3x - 10 = 0$, so $(x - 5)(x + 2) = 0$. The positive solution is $x = 5$.
mock-3-m1-14 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
The legs of a right triangle have lengths $9$ and $12$. What is the length of the hypotenuse?
  1. $13$
  2. $15$
  3. $17$
  4. $21$
Answer B. $\sqrt{9^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{81 + 144} = \sqrt{225} = 15$.
mock-3-m1-15 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 2
The line of best fit for a scatterplot is $y = 1.5x + 20$. What value of $y$ does the line predict when $x = 12$?
  1. $30$
  2. $34$
  3. $36$
  4. $38$
Answer D. $y = 1.5(12) + 20 = 18 + 20 = 38$.
mock-3-m1-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
A bag contains $5$ red, $7$ blue and $8$ green counters. One counter is chosen at random. What is the probability that it is not blue?
  1. $\dfrac{7}{20}$
  2. $\dfrac{2}{5}$
  3. $\dfrac{13}{20}$
  4. $\dfrac{3}{4}$
Answer C. There are $20$ counters and $5 + 8 = 13$ are not blue, so the probability is $\frac{13}{20}$.
mock-3-m1-17 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 3
A line passes through the points $(2, 5)$ and $(6, 17)$. What is the $y$-intercept of the line?
  1. $-1$
  2. $1$
  3. $3$
  4. $5$
Answer A. The slope is $\frac{17 - 5}{6 - 2} = 3$. Then $5 = 3(2) + b$, so $b = -1$.
mock-3-m1-18 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle in the $xy$-plane has the equation $x^2 + y^2 - 6x + 4y - 12 = 0$. What is the radius of the circle?
  1. $2\sqrt{3}$
  2. $5$
  3. $12$
  4. $25$
Answer B. Complete the square: $(x - 3)^2 - 9 + (y + 2)^2 - 4 - 12 = 0$, so $(x - 3)^2 + (y + 2)^2 = 25$. The radius is $\sqrt{25} = 5$.
mock-3-m1-19 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
Which expression is equivalent to $(2x + 1)^2 - (2x - 1)^2$?
  1. $0$
  2. $2$
  3. $4x$
  4. $8x$
Answer D. $(4x^2 + 4x + 1) - (4x^2 - 4x + 1) = 8x$.
mock-3-m1-20 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 3
A random sample of $400$ residents of a town was asked whether they support a new park. Of those surveyed, $62\%$ said yes, and the margin of error is $4$ percentage points. Which conclusion is most appropriate?
  1. Exactly 62% of all residents of the town support the park.
  2. It is plausible that between 58% and 66% of all residents of the town support the park.
  3. At least 66% of all residents of the town support the park.
  4. The results apply only to the 400 residents who were surveyed.
Answer B. A random sample with a margin of error of $4$ points gives a plausible range of $62 - 4 = 58\%$ to $62 + 4 = 66\%$ for the whole town. It does not give an exact value.
mock-3-m1-21 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A right circular cylinder has a radius of $3$ and a height of $8$. Its volume is $k\pi$. What is the value of $k$?

Type-in answer: 72

Answer 72. $V = \pi r^2 h = \pi(3^2)(8) = 72\pi$, so $k = 72$.
mock-3-m1-22 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = a \cdot 2^x$, where $a$ is a constant. If $f(3) = 40$, what is the value of $f(5)$?

Type-in answer: 160

Answer 160. $a \cdot 2^3 = 40$, so $a = 5$. Then $f(5) = 5 \cdot 2^5 = 5 \cdot 32 = 160$.

Math · Module 2 (easier) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-3-m2e-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $\dfrac{x}{4} = 7$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $3$
  2. $11$
  3. $24$
  4. $28$
Answer D. Multiply both sides by $4$: $x = 28$.
mock-3-m2e-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 1
Three pens cost $\$4.50$ in total. Each pen costs the same. What is the cost of one pen?
  1. $\$1.50$
  2. $\$1.75$
  3. $\$4.50$
  4. $\$13.50$
Answer A. $\$4.50 \div 3 = \$1.50$.
mock-3-m2e-03 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
What is $20\%$ of $85$?

Type-in answer: 17

Answer 17. $0.2 \times 85 = 17$.
mock-3-m2e-04 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 1
Two angles lie on a straight line. One measures $130^\circ$. What is the measure of the other?
  1. $50^\circ$
  2. $60^\circ$
  3. $130^\circ$
  4. $230^\circ$
Answer A. Angles on a straight line add to $180^\circ$: $180 - 130 = 50$.
mock-3-m2e-05 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 2x + 7$. What is the value of $f(0)$?
  1. $2$
  2. $7$
  3. $9$
  4. $14$
Answer B. $f(0) = 2(0) + 7 = 7$.
mock-3-m2e-06 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 1
Which expression is equivalent to $3(x + 5) - x$?
  1. $2x + 5$
  2. $2x + 15$
  3. $4x + 5$
  4. $4x + 15$
Answer B. $3x + 15 - x = 2x + 15$.
mock-3-m2e-07 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 1
What is the median of the data set $2, 9, 4, 7, 5$?
  1. $4$
  2. $4.5$
  3. $5$
  4. $5.4$
Answer C. In order the values are $2, 4, 5, 7, 9$, so the middle value is $5$. The value $5.4$ is the mean.
mock-3-m2e-08 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 1
The point $(3, k)$ lies on the line $y = 4x - 2$. What is the value of $k$?

Type-in answer: 10

Answer 10. Substitute $x = 3$: $k = 4(3) - 2 = 10$.
mock-3-m2e-09 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 1
A cube has edges of length $4$ centimetres. What is its volume, in cubic centimetres?
  1. $12$
  2. $16$
  3. $24$
  4. $64$
Answer D. The volume of a cube is the edge length cubed: $V = 4^3 = 4 \times 4 \times 4 = 64$.
mock-3-m2e-10 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 1
A fair six-sided die is rolled once. What is the probability of rolling an even number?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{6}$
  2. $\dfrac{1}{3}$
  3. $\dfrac{1}{2}$
  4. $\dfrac{2}{3}$
Answer C. Three of the six faces ($2, 4, 6$) are even, so the probability is $\frac{3}{6} = \frac{1}{2}$.
mock-3-m2e-11 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$y = 2x$
$x + y = 12$


What is the value of $x$ in the solution to the system of equations above?
  1. $4$
  2. $6$
  3. $8$
  4. $12$
Answer A. Substitute $y = 2x$: $x + 2x = 12$, so $3x = 12$ and $x = 4$.
mock-3-m2e-12 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = x^2 + 1$. What is the value of $f(-3)$?
  1. $-8$
  2. $-5$
  3. $7$
  4. $10$
Answer D. $f(-3) = (-3)^2 + 1 = 9 + 1 = 10$.
mock-3-m2e-13 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 2
The line of best fit for a set of data is $y = 0.5x + 3$. According to this model, what is the value of $y$ when $x = 10$?
  1. $3$
  2. $5$
  3. $6.5$
  4. $8$
Answer D. $y = 0.5(10) + 3 = 5 + 3 = 8$.
mock-3-m2e-14 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
Books cost $\$6$ each. What is the greatest number of books that can be bought with $\$50$?
  1. $6$
  2. $7$
  3. $8$
  4. $9$
Answer C. $6b \le 50$ gives $b \le 8.33$, so the greatest whole number is $8$.
mock-3-m2e-15 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
A right triangle has a hypotenuse of length $13$ and one leg of length $5$. What is the length of the other leg?

Type-in answer: 12

Answer 12. $\sqrt{13^2 - 5^2} = \sqrt{169 - 25} = \sqrt{144} = 12$.
mock-3-m2e-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
A jacket costs $\$80$. Its price is increased by $25\%$. What is the new price?
  1. $\$100$
  2. $\$105$
  3. $\$120$
  4. $\$125$
Answer A. $25\%$ of $80$ is $20$, so the new price is $80 + 20 = \$100$.
mock-3-m2e-17 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
If $x^2 = 81$ and $x > 0$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 9

Answer 9. The solutions are $x = 9$ and $x = -9$. Since $x > 0$, $x = 9$.
mock-3-m2e-18 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Studies and experiments · difficulty 2
A researcher randomly selected $200$ students from one school and randomly assigned half of them to a daily $10$-minute morning exercise programme. After a term, the exercise group had higher average test scores. Which conclusion is most appropriate?
  1. The programme likely caused higher test scores for students at that school.
  2. The programme would raise test scores for all students in the country.
  3. No conclusion about the programme can be drawn.
  4. Students with higher test scores chose to join the programme.
Answer A. Random assignment allows a conclusion about cause, and random selection from one school lets the result apply to that school only, not the whole country. The students did not choose their group.
mock-3-m2e-19 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 2
What is the slope of the line that passes through the points $(1, 3)$ and $(4, 12)$?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{3}$
  2. $1$
  3. $3$
  4. $9$
Answer C. Slope $= \frac{12 - 3}{4 - 1} = \frac{9}{3} = 3$.
mock-3-m2e-20 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 2
A circle has a radius of $6$. What is its circumference?
  1. $6\pi$
  2. $12\pi$
  3. $36\pi$
  4. $144\pi$
Answer B. Circumference $= 2\pi r = 2\pi(6) = 12\pi$. The value $36\pi$ is the area.
mock-3-m2e-21 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A runner covers $400$ metres in $80$ seconds. What is the runner's average speed, in metres per second?
  1. $0.2$
  2. $5$
  3. $20$
  4. $320$
Answer B. Speed $=$ distance $\div$ time $= 400 \div 80 = 5$.
mock-3-m2e-22 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
The expression $x^2 + 10x + 25$ is equivalent to $(x + k)^2$, where $k$ is a constant. What is the value of $k$?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. $(x + 5)^2 = x^2 + 10x + 25$, so $k = 5$.

Math · Module 2 (harder) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-3-m2h-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 2
If $3(x - 4) = 2x + 5$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $1$
  2. $9$
  3. $13$
  4. $17$
Answer D. Expand: $3x - 12 = 2x + 5$, so $x = 17$.
mock-3-m2h-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
An item costs $\$50$. Its price is decreased by $20\%$, and then the new price is increased by $20\%$. What is the final price?
  1. $\$48$
  2. $\$50$
  3. $\$52$
  4. $\$60$
Answer A. $50 \times 0.8 = 40$, then $40 \times 1.2 = 48$. The $20\%$ increase is taken of a smaller amount, so the price does not return to $\$50$.
mock-3-m2h-03 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $x^2 - 49$?
  1. $(x - 7)^2$
  2. $(x + 7)^2$
  3. $(x - 7)(x + 7)$
  4. $(x - 49)(x + 1)$
Answer C. This is a difference of two squares: $x^2 - 7^2 = (x - 7)(x + 7)$.
mock-3-m2h-04 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 3
$3x + ky = 8$
$6x + 10y = 5$


In the system of equations above, $k$ is a constant. For what value of $k$ does the system have no solution?
  1. $2$
  2. $5$
  3. $10$
  4. $20$
Answer B. No solution means the lines are parallel and distinct. The $x$-coefficients have ratio $\frac{3}{6} = \frac{1}{2}$, so $\frac{k}{10} = \frac{1}{2}$ and $k = 5$. The constants have ratio $\frac{8}{5}$, not $\frac{1}{2}$, so the lines are distinct.
mock-3-m2h-05 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
$y = x^2 - 4x + 7$
$y = 2x - 2$


The system of equations above has exactly one solution $(x, y)$. What is the value of $y$?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. Set equal: $x^2 - 4x + 7 = 2x - 2$, so $x^2 - 6x + 9 = 0$ and $(x - 3)^2 = 0$. Then $x = 3$ and $y = 2(3) - 2 = 4$.
mock-3-m2h-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 3
A town had a population of $2{,}000$ in 2020, and its population grows by $5\%$ each year. Which function models the population $P$ after $t$ years?
  1. $P = 2{,}000 + 0.05t$
  2. $P = 2{,}000(0.05)^t$
  3. $P = 2{,}000(1.5)^t$
  4. $P = 2{,}000(1.05)^t$
Answer D. Growth by $5\%$ each year multiplies the population by $1 + 0.05 = 1.05$ every year, so $P = 2{,}000(1.05)^t$.
mock-3-m2h-07 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle has a radius of $9$. What is the length of an arc of the circle with a central angle of $80^\circ$?
  1. $\pi$
  2. $2\pi$
  3. $4\pi$
  4. $8\pi$
Answer C. Arc length $= \frac{80}{360} \times 2\pi(9) = \frac{2}{9} \times 18\pi = 4\pi$.
mock-3-m2h-08 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = (x - 3)(x + 5)$. What is the minimum value of $f(x)$?
  1. $-16$
  2. $-15$
  3. $-1$
  4. $15$
Answer A. The zeros are $3$ and $-5$, so the vertex is midway at $x = -1$. Then $f(-1) = (-4)(4) = -16$.
mock-3-m2h-09 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
Of $120$ students, $45$ take art, $30$ take music and $12$ take both. A student who takes art is chosen at random. What is the probability that this student also takes music?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{10}$
  2. $\dfrac{1}{4}$
  3. $\dfrac{4}{15}$
  4. $\dfrac{2}{5}$
Answer C. Only the $45$ art students count, and $12$ of them take music: $\frac{12}{45} = \frac{4}{15}$.
mock-3-m2h-10 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 3
A plumber charges a $\$60$ call-out fee plus $\$45$ per hour. A customer can spend at most $\$300$. What is the greatest whole number of hours the plumber can work?
  1. $3$
  2. $4$
  3. $5$
  4. $6$
Answer C. $60 + 45h \le 300$ gives $45h \le 240$, so $h \le 5.33$. The greatest whole number is $5$.
mock-3-m2h-11 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 3
If $\sin(x^\circ) = \cos((2x - 30)^\circ)$ and $0 < x < 60$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 40

Answer 40. The sine of an angle equals the cosine of its complement, so $x + (2x - 30) = 90$. Then $3x = 120$ and $x = 40$.
mock-3-m2h-12 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
Which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{4x^2 - 9}{2x + 3}$, for $x \ne -\dfrac{3}{2}$?
  1. $2x - 3$
  2. $2x + 3$
  3. $2x - 9$
  4. $4x - 3$
Answer A. Factor the numerator as a difference of squares: $\frac{(2x - 3)(2x + 3)}{2x + 3} = 2x - 3$.
mock-3-m2h-13 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 3
A researcher surveys a random sample of $150$ students at a university about their weekly study hours and reports a margin of error. Which change would most likely reduce the margin of error?
  1. Surveying a larger random sample of students at the university
  2. Surveying only students in one class
  3. Surveying the same number of students, all from one subject
  4. Reporting the sample mean to more decimal places
Answer A. The margin of error falls as the size of a random sample grows. Narrowing the sample to one class or subject makes it less representative, and decimal places do not change the error.
mock-3-m2h-14 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 3
For the linear function $f$, $f(2) = 11$ and $f(6) = 23$. What is the value of $f(10)$?

Type-in answer: 35

Answer 35. The slope is $\frac{23 - 11}{6 - 2} = 3$, so $f(10) = f(6) + 3(4) = 23 + 12 = 35$.
mock-3-m2h-15 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
How many $x$-intercepts does the graph of $y = x^3 - 4x$ have?
  1. $0$
  2. $1$
  3. $2$
  4. $3$
Answer D. $x^3 - 4x = x(x - 2)(x + 2)$, which is zero at $x = -2, 0, 2$: three intercepts.
mock-3-m2h-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 3
A data set of $5$ numbers has a mean of $12$. When a sixth number is added, the mean becomes $13$. What is the sixth number?
  1. $13$
  2. $14$
  3. $16$
  4. $18$
Answer D. The first sum is $5 \times 12 = 60$ and the new sum is $6 \times 13 = 78$, so the sixth number is $78 - 60 = 18$.
mock-3-m2h-17 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A sphere has a volume of $36\pi$ cubic units. What is the radius of the sphere?

Type-in answer: 3

Answer 3. $\frac{4}{3}\pi r^3 = 36\pi$, so $r^3 = 27$ and $r = 3$.
mock-3-m2h-18 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 3
A tank fills at a rate of $12$ litres per minute. How many hours does it take to fill $2{,}160$ litres?
  1. $1.8$
  2. $3$
  3. $18$
  4. $180$
Answer B. $2{,}160 \div 12 = 180$ minutes, and $180 \div 60 = 3$ hours.
mock-3-m2h-19 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
What is the solution to the equation $\sqrt{x + 7} = x - 5$?

Type-in answer: 9

Answer 9. Square both sides: $x + 7 = x^2 - 10x + 25$, so $x^2 - 11x + 18 = 0$ and $x = 9$ or $x = 2$. Check: $x = 2$ gives $3 = -3$, which is false, so $x = 9$.
mock-3-m2h-20 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 3
Line $\ell$ is perpendicular to the line $4x - 3y = 12$. What is the slope of line $\ell$?
  1. $-\dfrac{4}{3}$
  2. $-\dfrac{3}{4}$
  3. $\dfrac{3}{4}$
  4. $\dfrac{4}{3}$
Answer B. Rewrite as $y = \frac{4}{3}x - 4$, so the slope is $\frac{4}{3}$. A perpendicular slope is the negative reciprocal, $-\frac{3}{4}$.
mock-3-m2h-21 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 3
In triangle $ABC$, point $D$ is on $AB$ and point $E$ is on $AC$, with $DE$ parallel to $BC$. If $AD = 4$, $DB = 6$ and $DE = 5$, what is the length of $BC$?
  1. $7.5$
  2. $10$
  3. $12.5$
  4. $15$
Answer C. Triangles $ADE$ and $ABC$ are similar, with $AB = 4 + 6 = 10$. So $\frac{BC}{5} = \frac{10}{4}$ and $BC = 12.5$.
mock-3-m2h-22 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 3
The number $a$ is $150\%$ of $b$, and $b$ is $40\%$ of $c$. The number $a$ is what percentage of $c$?
  1. $40\%$
  2. $60\%$
  3. $90\%$
  4. $190\%$
Answer B. $a = 1.5b = 1.5(0.4c) = 0.6c$, so $a$ is $60\%$ of $c$.

SAT Practice Test 4

Reading and Writing · Module 1 · 27 questions · 32 min

mock-4-rw1-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
The bakery's sourdough is famously ______: loaves bought on a Monday are often still soft on Friday.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. fragile
  2. long-lasting
  3. expensive
  4. rare
Answer B. Bread that stays soft from Monday to Friday keeps well for a long time, so "long-lasting" fits.
mock-4-rw1-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 1
Although the volcano had been ______ for centuries, scientists continued to monitor it, knowing that it could erupt again.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. famous
  2. active
  3. dormant
  4. visible
Answer C. "Although" and "could erupt again" show that the volcano had been quiet but not finished: "dormant". An "active" volcano would obviously need watching.
mock-4-rw1-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
The mayor's plan was ______ by the council, which voted unanimously to adopt it without changes.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. amended
  2. rejected
  3. postponed
  4. endorsed
Answer D. A unanimous vote to adopt the plan without changes means the council approved it: "endorsed". "Amended" contradicts "without changes".
mock-4-rw1-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
Historian Li Wei argues that the treaty's importance has been ______: although textbooks present it as a turning point, it changed little about how the two countries actually traded.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. overstated
  2. underestimated
  3. ignored
  4. disputed
Answer A. Textbooks call it a turning point, but Li says it changed little, so its importance has been exaggerated: "overstated".
mock-4-rw1-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 1
Octopuses can change the colour of their skin in less than a second. They do this using thousands of cells called chromatophores, each filled with pigment. Muscles around each cell stretch it open to show the colour or squeeze it shut to hide it.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To explain how octopuses are able to change colour so quickly
  2. To argue that octopuses are the most intelligent sea animals
  3. To compare octopuses with other animals that change colour
  4. To describe where octopuses live
Answer A. The text names the ability and then explains the cells and muscles that make it possible. It makes no comparison or argument about intelligence.
mock-4-rw1-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is from a short story.


The house had stood empty for years. Its windows were grey with dust, and nettles had swallowed the garden path. Yet when Clara pushed open the gate, she noticed a single rose blooming beside the door, as bright as if someone had tended it that morning.
Which choice best describes the function of the final sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It explains why the house had been left empty.
  2. It introduces a detail that contrasts with the neglect described earlier.
  3. It describes Clara's memories of living in the house.
  4. It suggests that Clara plans to repair the garden.
Answer B. The first sentences describe dust and nettles; "Yet" introduces a fresh, carefully kept rose, which contrasts with that neglect.
mock-4-rw1-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Some economists claim that working from home reduces productivity. A 2023 study of a travel company, however, found that employees allowed to work from home two days a week were just as productive as those in the office and were less likely to leave the company. The study's authors caution that the results may differ in jobs that require close teamwork.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It compares two companies' policies on working from home.
  2. It describes a study and then explains why its results are wrong.
  3. It presents a claim, describes a study that challenges it, and then notes a limitation of the study.
  4. It presents a claim and then gives several examples that support it.
Answer C. The text moves from the economists' claim, to the study that contradicts it ("however"), to the authors' warning about where the result may not apply.
mock-4-rw1-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
Many people assume that young children learn to speak mainly by imitating the adults around them. On this view, a child's grammar is simply a copy of the grammar the child hears.


Text 2
Linguist Ana Duarte points out that young English speakers often say forms such as "goed" and "mouses", which adults almost never say. Such errors, she argues, show that children work out general rules, like adding "-ed" for the past, and then apply them too widely.
Based on the texts, how would Duarte (Text 2) most likely respond to the view described in Text 1?
  1. By suggesting that adults should correct children's errors more often
  2. By agreeing that children copy exactly what adults say
  3. By claiming that children learn to speak only from books
  4. By arguing that imitation cannot explain errors showing that children apply grammatical rules of their own
Answer D. Children produce forms they never hear, such as "goed", so imitation alone cannot explain them; Duarte says children build and overapply rules.
mock-4-rw1-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 1
Monarch butterflies travel up to 4,000 kilometres each autumn, from Canada and the northern United States to forests in central Mexico. No single butterfly makes the round trip, however. The journey north in spring is completed by several generations, each of which lives only a few weeks.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Monarch butterflies live longer than most other insects.
  2. The monarch migration is so long that the return journey takes several generations to complete.
  3. Monarch butterflies spend the whole year in Mexico.
  4. Monarch butterflies travel only short distances.
Answer B. The text gives the long autumn journey and explains that the return trip north is finished by several short-lived generations.
mock-4-rw1-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is from a short story.


Grandfather kept every letter he had ever received in a tin box beneath his bed. As far as anyone knew, he never reread them. But when the river flooded the house, it was the tin box he carried upstairs first, before his coat or his shoes.
Based on the text, what is most strongly suggested about Grandfather?
  1. He was more worried about his coat than about the letters.
  2. He had forgotten that the letters were under his bed.
  3. He valued the letters deeply, even though he did not reread them.
  4. He planned to throw the letters away after the flood.
Answer C. Saving the box before his coat or shoes shows how much the letters mattered to him, whatever his reading habits.
mock-4-rw1-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Ecologist Marta Ruiz claims that planting community gardens increases the number of bee species found in a city neighbourhood.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Ruiz's claim?
  1. Community gardens often grow vegetables as well as flowers.
  2. Bees visit many kinds of flowers during a single day.
  3. Some city residents are afraid of being stung by bees.
  4. Neighbourhoods had more bee species after gardens were planted than before, while similar neighbourhoods without new gardens showed no change.
Answer D. The claim is that gardens increase bee species. A rise after planting, with no change in comparable areas without gardens, links the increase to the gardens.
mock-4-rw1-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 2
In the poem "The Lighthouse Keeper", the speaker conveys the keeper's loneliness.
Which quotation from the poem most effectively illustrates this claim?
  1. "No voice but the gulls' has spoken to me in a year"
  2. "The lamp turns, bright and steady, through the night"
  3. "The sea is calm and silver in the evening"
  4. "The ships pass safely by the rocks below"
Answer A. Hearing no voice but the gulls' for a year shows isolation. The other lines describe the lamp, the sea and the ships, not the keeper's feelings.
mock-4-rw1-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 1
Researchers measured the reaction times of students who had slept for different lengths of time the night before.


Hours of sleepAverage reaction time (milliseconds)
5320
6295
7270
8255
9250



The researchers found that more sleep was associated with faster reactions: ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to complete the statement?
  1. students who slept 9 hours reacted in 250 milliseconds on average, compared with 320 milliseconds for those who slept 5 hours.
  2. students who slept 7 hours reacted in 270 milliseconds on average.
  3. the slowest average reaction time was 320 milliseconds.
  4. some students slept for 8 hours.
Answer A. A shorter reaction time is faster. Comparing the most and least sleep shows more sleep going with a faster average reaction; the other choices give single values.
mock-4-rw1-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
A publisher recorded its book sales, in thousands of copies, by format.


Format20192023
Print640600
E-book180150
Audiobook60150



A manager claims that audiobooks were the only format whose sales grew between 2019 and 2023.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the manager's claim?
  1. Print sold the most copies in both years.
  2. Audiobook sales rose from 60,000 to 150,000 copies, while print and e-book sales both fell.
  3. E-book and audiobook sales were equal in 2023.
  4. Print sales fell by 40,000 copies.
Answer B. To support "the only format that grew", the data must show audiobooks rising and the other formats not rising. Only one choice shows both.
mock-4-rw1-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
In a study, plants grown with recordings of birdsong grew no differently from plants grown in silence. Plants placed near a fan that gently shook their stems, however, grew shorter but sturdier stems. The researchers concluded that the plants were responding to ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. the noise made by the fan.
  2. the songs of particular birds.
  3. physical movement rather than to sound.
  4. how much water they received.
Answer C. Sound alone (birdsong) made no difference, but shaking did, so the plants responded to movement. The fan's noise is ruled out because sound had no effect.
mock-4-rw1-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Archaeologists studying a village from about 800 BCE found that some of its pottery was made from clay dug from a riverbank 200 kilometres away, even though good clay was available nearby. Pots made with the distant clay were more elaborately decorated and were found mainly in burials, not in homes. This suggests that the distant clay ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. made pots that were stronger for everyday cooking.
  2. was cheaper than the clay found near the village.
  3. was used for most of the pots found in homes.
  4. was reserved for objects with special, possibly ceremonial, importance.
Answer D. The distant clay was used despite nearby clay, for finely decorated pots found mainly in burials. That points to special or ceremonial use, not everyday or cheaper use.
mock-4-rw1-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 1

  1. 1998,
  2. 1998;
  3. 1998
  4. 1998 and,
Answer B. Both parts are complete sentences, so they need a semicolon. A comma alone, or nothing, would join them incorrectly.

  4. Earle:
Answer C. "Known for her deep-sea explorations" is extra information set off by commas; the closing comma after "explorations" needs an opening comma after "Earle".

  4. blackbirds:

  4. Similarly
Answer A. Most birds fly, so a bird that cannot fly is a contrast with what is expected: "However".

mock-4-rw1-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The Atacama Desert in Chile is one of the driest places on Earth.
• Some weather stations there have never recorded rain.
• Its skies are unusually clear and dark.
• Several major observatories have been built there.
The student wants to explain why astronomers build observatories in the Atacama Desert. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The Atacama Desert in Chile is one of the driest places on Earth.
  2. Because its skies are unusually clear and dark, the Atacama Desert has become home to several major observatories.
  3. Some weather stations in the Atacama Desert have never recorded rain.
  4. Several major observatories have been built in Chile.
Answer B. Only one choice gives a reason (clear, dark skies) and connects it to the observatories.

  4. lost

Honeybees share the location of food through a "waggle dance". The angle of the dance shows the direction of the food relative to the sun, and the length of each waggle shows how far away it is.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To describe how honeybees use a dance to show other bees where food is
  2. To argue that honeybees are endangered
  3. To explain how honeybees make honey
  4. To compare honeybees with other insects
Answer A. Both sentences explain what the waggle dance shows: the food's direction and distance.

  1. It explains why deserts are hot.
  2. It gives an example that challenges a common assumption.
  3. It describes the animals that live in Antarctica.
  4. It supports the assumption stated in the first sentence.

  2. To describe the route the train will take
  3. To show a character's reluctance to let go of a farewell
  4. To introduce Amir's mother as the main character
Answer C. Amir keeps watching "long after there was nothing left to see", which shows he does not want the goodbye to end. The route and his reasons are not given.
mock-4-rw2e-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 2
Text 1
Some nutritionists recommend eating breakfast every day, arguing that it helps people control their weight.


Text 2
A review of randomised trials found that adults who were asked to eat breakfast did not lose more weight than those asked to skip it. In fact, the breakfast eaters consumed slightly more calories in total each day.
Based on the texts, how would the authors of the review in Text 2 most likely respond to the nutritionists in Text 1?
  1. By claiming that skipping breakfast causes weight gain
  2. By agreeing that everyone should eat breakfast
  3. By arguing that breakfast should be the largest meal of the day
  4. By pointing out that the evidence does not show breakfast helping people control their weight
Answer D. The trials found no extra weight loss from eating breakfast, which contradicts the nutritionists' reason for recommending it.

mock-4-rw2h-01 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 2
Critics praised the documentary for its ______ approach: it presented the views of farmers, scientists and government officials without favouring any of them.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. even-handed
  2. partisan
  3. sensational
  4. technical
Answer A. Presenting every group's views without favouring any is fair and balanced: "even-handed". "Partisan" means taking one side.
mock-4-rw2h-02 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3
The committee's report was deliberately ______, leaving each member free to interpret its recommendations as they wished.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. precise
  2. ambiguous
  3. lengthy
  4. confidential
Answer B. A report that lets each reader interpret it differently is open to more than one meaning: "ambiguous". A "precise" report would allow only one reading.
mock-4-rw2h-03 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3

  1. costly
  2. lasting
  3. passing
  4. practical
Answer C. The sentence contrasts how the bike was once seen with what it has become: a permanent fixture. The contrast needs a word meaning short-lived: "passing".
mock-4-rw2h-04 · Craft and Structure · Words in Context · difficulty 3

Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
  1. incomplete
  2. fictional
  3. irrelevant
  4. reliable
Answer D. "Although... errors" sets up a contrast: despite mistakes, the account can still be trusted on some matters: "reliable".
mock-4-rw2h-05 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 2
The following text is from a poem.


The city sleeps, but not the bakers' lights;
Their ovens glow through all the quiet nights,
So that, when dawn comes slowly up the street,
The town will find its bread still warm to eat.
Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
  1. It describes a baker's memories of childhood.
  2. It contrasts a noisy city with a quiet village.
  3. It describes bakers working through the night and then the result that the town enjoys in the morning.
  4. It explains how bread is made, step by step.
Answer C. The first two lines show the bakers working while the city sleeps; "So that" leads to the result, warm bread for the town at dawn.
mock-4-rw2h-06 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
For centuries, Roman harbour walls puzzled engineers: modern concrete can crumble in seawater within decades, yet Roman structures have lasted two thousand years. A 2017 study traced the difference to the volcanic ash the Romans mixed into their concrete. Seawater seeping into the walls reacts with the ash to grow new minerals that strengthen the concrete over time.
Which choice best describes the function of the final sentence in the text as a whole?
  1. It suggests that Roman walls are now crumbling.
  2. It questions whether the 2017 study was accurate.
  3. It describes how modern concrete is made.
  4. It explains the process responsible for the durability described earlier.
Answer D. The text sets up a puzzle (why Roman concrete lasts), names the ash as the cause, and the final sentence explains how the ash and seawater make it stronger.
mock-4-rw2h-07 · Craft and Structure · Text Structure and Purpose · difficulty 3
Biologist Tomas Reyes notes that crows can solve puzzles that require several steps in the right order. He cautions, though, against concluding from this that crows reason as humans do. Similar results, he points out, have been produced by animals following simple learned rules, and the experiments so far cannot tell the two explanations apart.
Which choice best states the main purpose of the text?
  1. To present a researcher's warning against drawing too strong a conclusion from crows' puzzle-solving
  2. To argue that crows are more intelligent than humans
  3. To describe the steps of a puzzle that crows solve
  4. To show that crows cannot solve puzzles
Answer A. Reyes accepts that crows solve the puzzles but warns that the results do not show human-like reasoning, because simpler explanations have not been ruled out.
mock-4-rw2h-08 · Craft and Structure · Cross-Text Connections · difficulty 3
Text 1
For decades, many urban planners held that adding lanes to busy roads was the most direct way to reduce traffic congestion. More space for cars, they argued, would mean fewer cars per lane.


Text 2
Economists studying cities in the United States found that when road capacity increased, the total distance driven rose almost in proportion, leaving congestion largely unchanged. New lanes, they concluded, attract new trips, an effect they call "induced demand".
Based on the texts, how would the economists in Text 2 most likely respond to the planners' view in Text 1?
  1. By agreeing that adding lanes is the best way to reduce congestion
  2. By arguing that extra lanes encourage more driving, so congestion does not fall as the planners expect
  3. By claiming that cities should build no new roads at all
  4. By suggesting that congestion is caused mainly by road repairs
Answer B. The economists found driving rose with capacity, so congestion stayed the same: the opposite of the planners' prediction. They do not say no roads should ever be built.
mock-4-rw2h-09 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 2
The following text is from a short story.


Mrs. Hale had lived on the street for forty years and had an opinion about all of it. She disliked the new streetlights, which she said kept her awake, and the new neighbours, who she said were far too loud. Yet every afternoon at four o'clock, she set out two cups of tea on her front step, in case anyone should stop to talk.
Which choice best describes Mrs. Hale as she is presented in the text?
  1. She has no interest in talking to anyone.
  2. She plans to move away from the street.
  3. She enjoys the noise made by her neighbours.
  4. Although she complains about her surroundings, she hopes for company.
Answer D. She criticises the lights and the neighbours, but "Yet" introduces the second cup of tea set out for visitors, which shows she wants company.
mock-4-rw2h-10 · Information and Ideas · Central Ideas and Details · difficulty 3
Glaciers are often described as slow-moving ice, but their movement is far from uniform. Some glaciers periodically "surge", advancing ten to a hundred times faster than usual for months or even years before slowing again. Researchers think that surges begin when meltwater builds up beneath a glacier, lifting it slightly off its bed and reducing friction.
Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
  1. Some glaciers periodically move much faster than normal, probably because meltwater reduces friction beneath them.
  2. All glaciers move at the same slow, steady speed.
  3. Glaciers are melting faster than ever before.
  4. Meltwater causes glaciers to stop moving entirely.
Answer A. The text says glacier movement is not uniform, describes surges, and gives the likely cause, meltwater lowering friction.
mock-4-rw2h-11 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Psychologist Ines Navarro hypothesises that people give more to charity when they are shown a photograph of a single person in need than when they are shown statistics about many people in need.
Which finding, if true, would most directly support Navarro's hypothesis?
  1. Most participants said that they gave to charity at least once a year.
  2. Participants shown a photograph of one child donated about twice as much, on average, as participants shown figures about millions of children.
  3. Participants shown statistics remembered more numbers than participants shown a photograph.
  4. Charities often use both photographs and statistics in their advertising.
Answer B. The hypothesis compares giving after a single photo with giving after statistics. Only the finding showing higher donations after the photo makes that comparison.
mock-4-rw2h-12 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Textual · difficulty 3
Some biologists hypothesise that the bright feathers of a certain bird species evolved mainly to help males attract mates.
Which finding, if true, would most directly weaken this hypothesis?
  1. The species lives in forests with many other brightly coloured birds.
  2. Males with the brightest feathers are chosen by females more often than other males.
  3. Males and females of the species have equally bright feathers, and brightness has no effect on which birds pair up.
  4. Bright feathers take a lot of energy for birds to grow.
Answer C. If brightness does not affect pairing, and females are just as bright as males, attracting mates is unlikely to be the main reason for the feathers. One choice supports the hypothesis instead.
mock-4-rw2h-13 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
Researchers recorded the share of households with solar panels in four regions.


Region20152022
North4%9%
South6%18%
East5%11%
West7%12%



The researchers claim that the South saw the largest increase in solar panel use between 2015 and 2022.
Which choice best describes data from the table that support the researchers' claim?
  1. The South's share rose by 12 percentage points, while the other regions' shares rose by 5 or 6 points.
  2. The West had the highest share in 2015.
  3. The North had the lowest share in both years.
  4. The East's share more than doubled.
Answer A. The claim is about the size of the increase. The South rose 12 points; the North and West rose 5 and the East 6.
mock-4-rw2h-14 · Information and Ideas · Command of Evidence: Quantitative · difficulty 3
An agricultural scientist tested how much fertiliser to add to plots of beans.


Fertiliser per plot (g)Average yield (kg)
04.0
105.5
206.2
306.3



The scientist concludes that beyond a certain amount, extra fertiliser adds little to the yield. ______
Which choice most effectively uses data from the table to support the scientist's conclusion?
  1. The plots with 30 g of fertiliser had the highest yield.
  2. Increasing the fertiliser from 20 g to 30 g raised the yield by only 0.1 kg, compared with 1.5 kg from 0 g to 10 g.
  3. Plots with no fertiliser had a yield of 4.0 kg.
  4. The yield with 10 g of fertiliser was 5.5 kg.
Answer B. The conclusion is about shrinking gains. Comparing the large gain from the first 10 g with the tiny gain from the last 10 g shows it.
mock-4-rw2h-15 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 3
Linguists have found that languages spoken in high mountain regions are more likely than others to include ejective consonants, sounds made with a sharp burst of air from the throat. One proposed explanation is that these sounds take less effort to produce where air pressure is lower. If this explanation is correct, then, all else being equal, ejective consonants should be ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. harder to produce at high altitudes than at low altitudes.
  2. found only in languages spoken in mountains.
  3. less common in languages spoken at low altitudes.
  4. unrelated to the altitude at which a language is spoken.
Answer C. If low air pressure makes ejectives easier, they should be rarer where air pressure is higher, at low altitudes. "Only in mountains" goes further than the explanation.
mock-4-rw2h-16 · Information and Ideas · Inferences · difficulty 2
A city replaced its parking meters with an app that charged higher prices on its busiest streets. Within a year, the number of drivers circling those streets in search of a space fell by a third, while the city's total income from parking stayed about the same. The results suggest that the higher prices ______
Which choice most logically completes the text?
  1. had no effect on where drivers chose to park.
  2. made the busiest streets completely empty of cars.
  3. caused the city to lose most of its parking income.
  4. led some drivers to park elsewhere or at other times, freeing up spaces without reducing the city's income.
Answer D. Fewer drivers circling means spaces became easier to find, so some drivers changed where or when they parked; income stayed level, so it did not fall.
mock-4-rw2h-17 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 2
The Rosetta Stone, discovered in ______ allowed scholars to decipher Egyptian hieroglyphs for the first time.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. 1799,
  2. 1799
  3. 1799;
  4. 1799:
Answer A. "Discovered in 1799" is extra information that opens with a comma after "Stone", so it must close with a comma before the verb "allowed".
mock-4-rw2h-18 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
The festival offers something for everyone: concerts for music lovers; workshops for ______ and, for the youngest visitors, a puppet theatre.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. artists,
  2. artists;
  3. artists:
  4. artists
Answer B. The list items contain commas, so they are separated by semicolons, as after "music lovers". A semicolon is needed after "artists" too.
mock-4-rw2h-19 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
Physicist Chien-Shiung Wu's 1956 experiment overturned a long-held ______ that nature does not distinguish between left and right.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. assumption;
  2. assumption,
  3. assumption
  4. assumption:
Answer C. The "that" clause states what the assumption was and is essential to the sentence, so no punctuation separates it from "assumption".
mock-4-rw2h-20 · Standard English Conventions · Boundaries · difficulty 3
Some of the world's oldest living trees are bristlecone ______ one of them, in California, is more than 4,800 years old.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. pines that
  2. pines,
  3. pines
  4. pines;
Answer D. Both parts are complete sentences, so a semicolon is needed. A comma alone, or nothing, would join them incorrectly.
mock-4-rw2h-21 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
The number of students taking the exam ______ risen every year since 2015.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. has
  2. have
  3. are
  4. were
Answer A. The subject is "The number", which is singular; "of students" describes it. So the singular "has" is needed.
mock-4-rw2h-22 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 3
The prize for the best science project was shared by my sister and ______
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. I.
  2. me.
  3. myself.
  4. mine.
Answer B. After the preposition "by", the object pronoun is needed. Removing "my sister and" gives "shared by me", so "me" is correct.
mock-4-rw2h-23 · Standard English Conventions · Form, Structure, and Sense · difficulty 2
Of the two proposals, the council chose the ______ expensive one.
Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?
  1. lesser
  2. least
  3. less
  4. lessest
Answer C. When comparing exactly two things, the comparative "less" is used; "least" is for three or more.
mock-4-rw2h-24 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 3
Many early critics dismissed photography as a mechanical process with no claim to be art. ______, by the early twentieth century, photographs were being shown in major galleries alongside paintings.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. Likewise
  2. Accordingly
  3. For instance
  4. Nevertheless
Answer D. Galleries showing photographs goes against the critics' dismissal, so a contrast transition, "Nevertheless", fits.
mock-4-rw2h-25 · Expression of Ideas · Transitions · difficulty 3
The drug lowered blood pressure in nearly every patient in the trial. ______, a small number of patients experienced dizziness severe enough that they stopped taking it.
Which choice completes the text with the most logical transition?
  1. That said
  2. Therefore
  3. In other words
  4. For example
Answer A. The second sentence adds a drawback that qualifies the success described first, so "That said" fits. The dizziness is not a restatement or an example of lower blood pressure.
mock-4-rw2h-26 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 2
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• The okapi lives in the rainforests of the Democratic Republic of the Congo.
• It has striped legs that resemble a zebra's.
• Its closest living relative is the giraffe.
• Scientists outside Africa first described it in 1901.
The student wants to emphasise a surprising fact about the okapi's relatives. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. The okapi lives in the rainforests of the Democratic Republic of the Congo.
  2. Although its striped legs resemble a zebra's, the okapi's closest living relative is the giraffe.
  3. Scientists outside Africa first described the okapi in 1901.
  4. The okapi has striped legs.
Answer B. The surprise is the contrast between zebra-like stripes and its true relative, the giraffe. Only one choice makes that contrast.
mock-4-rw2h-27 · Expression of Ideas · Rhetorical Synthesis · difficulty 3
While researching a topic, a student has taken the following notes:
• A 2010 study of 300 adults linked drinking coffee to a lower risk of liver disease.
• A 2018 study of 9,000 adults found the same link.
• Both were observational studies, not experiments.
• Observational studies can show links but cannot show that one thing causes another.
The student wants to present the studies' findings while acknowledging a limitation. Which choice most effectively uses relevant information from the notes to accomplish this goal?
  1. A 2018 study included 9,000 adults.
  2. Drinking coffee prevents liver disease, according to two studies.
  3. Two studies, one of 9,000 adults, linked coffee to a lower risk of liver disease, though as observational studies they cannot show that coffee causes the lower risk.
  4. Observational studies are different from experiments.
Answer C. The goal has two parts: the finding and its limitation. Only one choice gives both; another overstates the finding as proof that coffee prevents disease.

Math · Module 1 · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-4-m1-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $5x - 8 = 27$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $6$
  2. $7$
  3. $8$
  4. $9$
Answer B. Add $8$: $5x = 35$, so $x = 7$.
mock-4-m1-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
What is $30\%$ of $70$?
  1. $21$
  2. $30$
  3. $40$
  4. $49$
Answer A. $0.3 \times 70 = 21$.
mock-4-m1-03 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 1
In an isosceles triangle, each of the two equal angles measures $50^\circ$. What is the measure of the third angle?
  1. $50^\circ$
  2. $80^\circ$
  3. $100^\circ$
  4. $130^\circ$
Answer B. The angles add to $180^\circ$: $180 - 50 - 50 = 80$.
mock-4-m1-04 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = x^2 + 2x$. What is the value of $f(4)$?

Type-in answer: 24

Answer 24. $f(4) = 4^2 + 2(4) = 16 + 8 = 24$.
mock-4-m1-05 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
What is the slope of the line with equation $y = -2x + 9$?
  1. $-2$
  2. $2$
  3. $7$
  4. $9$
Answer A. In $y = mx + b$, the slope is $m$, so the slope is $-2$. The value $9$ is the $y$-intercept.
mock-4-m1-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A recipe uses $3$ cups of flour to make $12$ cookies. At this rate, how many cups of flour are needed to make $30$ cookies?
  1. $5$
  2. $6$
  3. $6.5$
  4. $7.5$
Answer D. Each cookie needs $\frac{3}{12} = 0.25$ cups, so $30$ cookies need $30 \times 0.25 = 7.5$ cups.
mock-4-m1-07 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$2x + y = 10$
$x + y = 7$


What is the value of $x$ in the solution to the system of equations above?

Type-in answer: 3

Answer 3. Subtract the second equation from the first: $x = 3$ (and then $y = 4$).
mock-4-m1-08 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 2
A triangle has a base of $10$ inches and a height of $7$ inches. What is its area, in square inches?
  1. $17$
  2. $20$
  3. $24$
  4. $35$
Answer D. Area $= \frac{1}{2} \times 10 \times 7 = 35$.
mock-4-m1-09 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $2x(x - 3) + 4x$?
  1. $2x^2 - 2x$
  2. $2x^2 + 2x$
  3. $2x^2 - 10x$
  4. $6x^2 - 2x$
Answer A. $2x^2 - 6x + 4x = 2x^2 - 2x$.
mock-4-m1-10 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 2
For the data set $6, 6, 8, 10, 15$, what is the mean minus the median?
  1. $0$
  2. $1$
  3. $2$
  4. $3$
Answer B. The mean is $\frac{45}{5} = 9$ and the median is the middle value, $8$. So $9 - 8 = 1$.
mock-4-m1-11 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
Which of the following describes all solutions to $-3x + 4 < 13$?
  1. $x > -3$
  2. $x < -3$
  3. $x > 3$
  4. $x < 3$
Answer A. Subtract $4$: $-3x < 9$. Dividing by $-3$ reverses the inequality: $x > -3$.
mock-4-m1-12 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
What is the sum of the solutions to $x^2 - 7x + 12 = 0$?
  1. $-7$
  2. $-1$
  3. $7$
  4. $12$
Answer C. $(x - 3)(x - 4) = 0$ gives $x = 3$ and $x = 4$, whose sum is $7$.
mock-4-m1-13 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
In a right triangle, one angle measures $30^\circ$ and the hypotenuse has length $14$. What is the length of the side opposite the $30^\circ$ angle?
  1. $7$
  2. $7\sqrt{3}$
  3. $14$
  4. $14\sqrt{3}$
Answer A. $\sin 30^\circ = \frac{1}{2}$, so the opposite side is $\frac{1}{2} \times 14 = 7$.
mock-4-m1-14 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 2
A spinner has $8$ equal sections, and $3$ of them are red. What is the probability that the spinner lands on red?

Type-in answer: 3/8 or .375 or 0.375

Answer 3/8. Three of the eight equal sections are red, so the probability is $\frac{3}{8} = 0.375$.
mock-4-m1-15 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 2
A shop sells notebooks for $\$3$ each and pens for $\$2$ each. A customer spends $\$24$ on $n$ notebooks and $6$ pens. What is the value of $n$?
  1. $2$
  2. $3$
  3. $4$
  4. $6$
Answer C. $3n + 2(6) = 24$, so $3n = 12$ and $n = 4$.
mock-4-m1-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 3
The equation $y = 250 - 12x$ models the water level $y$, in centimetres, of a pond $x$ days after a dry spell began. What is the best interpretation of $12$ in this context?
  1. The water level at the start was 12 centimetres.
  2. The water level rises by 12 centimetres each day.
  3. The dry spell lasted 12 days.
  4. The water level falls by 12 centimetres each day.
Answer D. $12$ is the coefficient of $x$ with a negative sign, so each extra day lowers $y$ by $12$: the level falls $12$ cm a day. The starting level is $250$ cm.
mock-4-m1-17 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 3
What is the vertex of the graph of $f(x) = 3(x - 2)^2 + 5$ in the $xy$-plane?
  1. $(-2, 5)$
  2. $(2, -5)$
  3. $(2, 5)$
  4. $(3, 5)$
Answer C. In $a(x - h)^2 + k$ form, the vertex is $(h, k) = (2, 5)$.
mock-4-m1-18 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 3
A circle in the $xy$-plane has its centre at $(0, 0)$ and passes through the point $(5, 12)$. What is the radius of the circle?
  1. $5$
  2. $12$
  3. $13$
  4. $17$
Answer C. The radius is the distance from $(0, 0)$ to $(5, 12)$: $\sqrt{5^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{169} = 13$.
mock-4-m1-19 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 3
A linear function $f$ has a slope of $4$, and $f(3) = 2$. What is the value of $f(0)$?

Type-in answer: -10

Answer -10. Going from $x = 3$ back to $x = 0$ lowers $f$ by $3 \times 4 = 12$, so $f(0) = 2 - 12 = -10$.
mock-4-m1-20 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Studies and experiments · difficulty 3
A researcher asked $500$ people at a town's gym how often they exercise, and $70\%$ said every day. Can the researcher conclude that about $70\%$ of all the town's residents exercise every day?
  1. Yes, because the sample is large.
  2. Yes, because 70% is more than half.
  3. No, because 500 people is too few to draw any conclusion.
  4. No, because the sample was taken only at a gym and was not chosen at random from all residents.
Answer D. People at a gym are likely to exercise more than other residents, so the sample is not representative of the town. A large sample does not fix a biased one.
mock-4-m1-21 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 3
Two parallel lines are cut by a transversal. A pair of alternate interior angles measure $(4x + 20)^\circ$ and $(2x + 40)^\circ$. What is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 10

Answer 10. Alternate interior angles are equal: $4x + 20 = 2x + 40$, so $2x = 20$ and $x = 10$.
mock-4-m1-22 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
For $x > 0$, which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{(x^{1/2})^6}{x}$?
  1. $x$
  2. $x^2$
  3. $x^3$
  4. $x^5$
Answer B. $(x^{1/2})^6 = x^3$, and $\frac{x^3}{x} = x^2$.

Math · Module 2 (easier) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-4-m2e-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 1
If $x + 15 = 42$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $3$
  2. $27$
  3. $37$
  4. $57$
Answer B. Subtract $15$ from both sides: $x = 42 - 15 = 27$.
mock-4-m2e-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 1
A printer prints $20$ pages per minute. How many minutes does it take to print $140$ pages?
  1. $7$
  2. $8$
  3. $9$
  4. $10$
Answer A. $140 \div 20 = 7$ minutes.
mock-4-m2e-03 · Algebra · Linear functions · difficulty 1
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 5x + 1$. What is the value of $f(2)$?

Type-in answer: 11

Answer 11. $f(2) = 5(2) + 1 = 11$.
mock-4-m2e-04 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 1
A square has sides of length $7$ feet. What is its area, in square feet?
  1. $14$
  2. $28$
  3. $42$
  4. $49$
Answer D. Area $= 7^2 = 49$. The value $28$ is the perimeter.
mock-4-m2e-05 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 1
In a class of $50$ students, $40\%$ walk to school. How many students walk to school?
  1. $10$
  2. $20$
  3. $25$
  4. $40$
Answer B. $0.4 \times 50 = 20$.
mock-4-m2e-06 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 1
Which expression is equivalent to $4x + 3x - 2$?
  1. $5x$
  2. $7x - 2$
  3. $7x + 2$
  4. $12x - 2$
Answer B. Combine like terms: $4x + 3x = 7x$, so the expression is $7x - 2$.
mock-4-m2e-07 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · One-variable data · difficulty 1
What is the mean of the data set $10, 20, 30, 40$?

Type-in answer: 25

Answer 25. The sum is $100$ and there are $4$ values, so the mean is $100 \div 4 = 25$.
mock-4-m2e-08 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Lines, angles and triangles · difficulty 1
A right angle is divided into two angles. One of them measures $35^\circ$. What is the measure of the other?
  1. $35^\circ$
  2. $45^\circ$
  3. $55^\circ$
  4. $145^\circ$
Answer C. A right angle is $90^\circ$, so the other angle is $90 - 35 = 55$.
mock-4-m2e-09 · Algebra · Linear equations in two variables · difficulty 1
Which point lies on the line $y = x + 4$?
  1. $(0, 0)$
  2. $(1, 4)$
  3. $(3, 5)$
  4. $(2, 6)$
Answer D. Substitute $x = 2$: $y = 2 + 4 = 6$, so $(2, 6)$ is on the line. The others do not fit: $0 \ne 4$, $4 \ne 5$ and $5 \ne 7$.
mock-4-m2e-10 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 1
A jar contains $4$ red marbles and $6$ white marbles. One marble is taken at random. What is the probability that it is red?
  1. $\dfrac{1}{4}$
  2. $\dfrac{2}{5}$
  3. $\dfrac{3}{5}$
  4. $\dfrac{2}{3}$
Answer B. There are $10$ marbles and $4$ are red: $\frac{4}{10} = \frac{2}{5}$.
mock-4-m2e-11 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 2
$x = y + 2$
$x + y = 10$


What is the value of $y$ in the solution to the system of equations above?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. Substitute: $(y + 2) + y = 10$, so $2y = 8$ and $y = 4$ (and $x = 6$).
mock-4-m2e-12 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $g$ is defined by $g(x) = x^2 - 4$. What is the value of $g(5)$?
  1. $1$
  2. $6$
  3. $21$
  4. $29$
Answer C. $g(5) = 5^2 - 4 = 25 - 4 = 21$.
mock-4-m2e-13 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 2
For a linear relationship, when $x = 1, 2, 3, 4$, the values of $y$ are $5, 8, 11, 14$. Which equation represents this relationship?
  1. $y = 3x + 2$
  2. $y = 2x + 3$
  3. $y = 5x$
  4. $y = x + 4$
Answer A. $y$ rises by $3$ each time $x$ rises by $1$, so the slope is $3$. Then $5 = 3(1) + b$ gives $b = 2$.
mock-4-m2e-14 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 2
A phone plan costs $\$15$ a month plus $\$0.10$ per text message. Mia wants to spend at most $\$25$ this month. What is the greatest number of text messages she can send?
  1. $10$
  2. $25$
  3. $50$
  4. $100$
Answer D. $15 + 0.1t \le 25$ gives $0.1t \le 10$, so $t \le 100$.
mock-4-m2e-15 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Right triangles and trigonometry · difficulty 2
In right triangle $ABC$, angle $C$ is the right angle and $\sin A = \dfrac{3}{5}$. What is $\cos A$?
  1. $\dfrac{4}{5}$
  2. $\dfrac{5}{4}$
  3. $\dfrac{4}{3}$
  4. $\dfrac{5}{3}$
Answer A. With opposite side $3$ and hypotenuse $5$, the adjacent side is $\sqrt{5^2 - 3^2} = 4$, so $\cos A = \frac{4}{5}$.
mock-4-m2e-16 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Percentages · difficulty 2
The price of a ticket fell from $\$40$ to $\$30$. By what percentage did the price decrease?
  1. $10\%$
  2. $25\%$
  3. $33\tfrac{1}{3}\%$
  4. $75\%$
Answer B. The decrease is $\$10$, and $\frac{10}{40} = 0.25 = 25\%$. The change is compared with the original price, $\$40$.
mock-4-m2e-17 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 2
If $x^2 - 16 = 0$ and $x > 0$, what is the value of $x$?

Type-in answer: 4

Answer 4. $x^2 = 16$, so $x = 4$ or $x = -4$. Since $x > 0$, $x = 4$.
mock-4-m2e-18 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Sample statistics and margin of error · difficulty 2
A school has $2{,}000$ students. In a random sample of $80$ of them, $20$ walk to school. Based on the sample, about how many of the school's students walk to school?
  1. $20$
  2. $80$
  3. $400$
  4. $500$
Answer D. $\frac{20}{80} = 25\%$ of the sample walk, and $25\%$ of $2{,}000$ is $500$.
mock-4-m2e-19 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 2
If $2(x + 3) = 18$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $6$
  2. $7.5$
  3. $9$
  4. $12$
Answer A. Divide by $2$: $x + 3 = 9$, so $x = 6$.
mock-4-m2e-20 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Circles · difficulty 2
A circle has an area of $25\pi$ square units. What is the radius of the circle?

Type-in answer: 5

Answer 5. $\pi r^2 = 25\pi$, so $r^2 = 25$ and $r = 5$.
mock-4-m2e-21 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
One inch is $2.54$ centimetres. How many centimetres are in $10$ inches?
  1. $3.94$
  2. $12.54$
  3. $25.4$
  4. $254$
Answer C. $10 \times 2.54 = 25.4$ centimetres.
mock-4-m2e-22 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 2
Which expression is equivalent to $(x + 3)^2$?
  1. $x^2 + 9$
  2. $x^2 + 3x + 9$
  3. $x^2 + 6x + 9$
  4. $x^2 + 6x + 6$
Answer C. $(x + 3)(x + 3) = x^2 + 3x + 3x + 9 = x^2 + 6x + 9$.

Math · Module 2 (harder) · 22 questions · 35 min

mock-4-m2h-01 · Algebra · Linear equations in one variable · difficulty 2
If $\dfrac{x}{3} + \dfrac{x}{6} = 5$, what is the value of $x$?
  1. $5$
  2. $10$
  3. $15$
  4. $30$
Answer B. $\frac{x}{3} = \frac{2x}{6}$, so the left side is $\frac{3x}{6} = \frac{x}{2}$. Then $\frac{x}{2} = 5$ and $x = 10$.
mock-4-m2h-02 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Ratios, rates and units · difficulty 2
A car uses $6$ litres of fuel per $100$ kilometres. Fuel costs $\$1.50$ per litre. What is the cost of the fuel for a $250$-kilometre trip?
  1. $\$22.50$
  2. $\$25.00$
  3. $\$30.00$
  4. $\$37.50$
Answer A. The trip uses $6 \times 2.5 = 15$ litres, and $15 \times \$1.50 = \$22.50$.
mock-4-m2h-03 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear functions · difficulty 2
The function $f$ is defined by $f(x) = 2^x + 3$. What is the value of $f(4)$?
  1. $11$
  2. $16$
  3. $19$
  4. $35$
Answer C. $f(4) = 2^4 + 3 = 16 + 3 = 19$.
mock-4-m2h-04 · Algebra · Systems of linear equations · difficulty 3
$ax + 2y = 6$
$3x + 6y = 18$


In the system of equations above, $a$ is a constant. For what value of $a$ does the system have infinitely many solutions?
  1. $1$
  2. $2$
  3. $3$
  4. $6$
Answer A. Dividing the second equation by $3$ gives $x + 2y = 6$. The two equations are the same line when $a = 1$.
mock-4-m2h-05 · Advanced Math · Nonlinear equations and systems · difficulty 3
The equation $x^2 + bx + 36 = 0$, where $b$ is a positive constant, has exactly one real solution. What is the value of $b$?

Type-in answer: 12

Answer 12. One real solution means the discriminant is zero: $b^2 - 4(36) = 0$, so $b^2 = 144$ and, since $b > 0$, $b = 12$.
mock-4-m2h-06 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Two-variable data and models · difficulty 3
The value $V$, in dollars, of a car $t$ years after it was bought is modelled by $V = 24{,}000(0.85)^t$. What is the best interpretation of $0.85$ in this context?
  1. The car's value decreases by 15% each year.
  2. The car's value decreases by 85% each year.
  3. The car's value increases by 85% each year.
  4. The car's value decreases by $0.85 each year.
Answer A. Multiplying by $0.85$ each year keeps $85\%$ of the value, which is a decrease of $100\% - 85\% = 15\%$ a year.
mock-4-m2h-07 · Geometry and Trigonometry · Area and volume · difficulty 3
A right circular cone has a radius of $3$ and a height of $4$. What is the volume of the cone?
  1. $4\pi$
  2. $12\pi$
  3. $36\pi$
  4. $48\pi$
Answer B. $V = \frac{1}{3}\pi r^2 h = \frac{1}{3}\pi(9)(4) = 12\pi$.
mock-4-m2h-08 · Advanced Math · Equivalent expressions · difficulty 3
For $x > 1$, which expression is equivalent to $\dfrac{1}{x - 1} - \dfrac{1}{x + 1}$?
  1. $\dfrac{2}{x^2 - 1}$
  2. $0$
  3. $\dfrac{2x}{x^2 - 1}$
  4. $-\dfrac{2}{x^2 - 1}$
Answer A. Use the common denominator $(x - 1)(x + 1) = x^2 - 1$: $\frac{(x + 1) - (x - 1)}{x^2 - 1} = \frac{2}{x^2 - 1}$.
mock-4-m2h-09 · Problem-Solving and Data Analysis · Probability · difficulty 3
In a survey of $150$ people, $102$ said they like a new logo. Of the people who like the logo, $48$ are under $18$. If one person who likes the logo is chosen at random, what is the probability that the person is under $18$?
  1. $\dfrac{8}{25}$
  2. $\dfrac{2}{5}$
  3. $\dfrac{8}{17}$
  4. $\dfrac{4}{5}$
Answer C. Only the $102$ people who like the logo count: $\frac{48}{102} = \frac{8}{17}$. Dividing by $150$ would use everyone surveyed.
mock-4-m2h-10 · Algebra · Linear inequalities · difficulty 3
$y \le 2x + 1$
$y \ge -x + 4$
